It manages tenant resolution (via domain, header, or user), database connection switching, and scope isolation for shared Hub services. It ensures that data from Tenant A never leaks into Tenant B.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nRelies on `HUB-01` (Config) and `HUB-04` (Identity). It must be in place before building any tenant-aware Spoke applications.\n\n## Context7 Research\n- **Direct Hub Dependencies**: `HUB-01: Global Config`, `HUB-04: Identity`, `HUB-08: API Gateway`.\n- **Transitive Core Dependencies**: `CORE-19: DBAL`, `CORE-10: Config`, `CORE-02: DI Container`.\n- **Patterns**: Database-per-tenant, Schema-per-tenant, or Column-based isolation. Implements Column-based as default with support for DB-per-tenant.\n\n## Architectural Design\n- **TenantResolver**: Identifies the current tenant context from the Request.\n- **TenantScope**: A global state object that holds the current tenant's ID and configuration.\n- **ConnectionSwitcher**: Automatically points `CORE-19` to the tenant's specific database if configured.\n- **StorageIsolation**: Ensures `HUB-11` file paths are prefixed with the Tenant ID.\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### TenancyInterface\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\Hub\\Contracts;\n\ninterface TenancyInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Get the current active tenant.\n     */\n    public function current(): ?Tenant;\n\n    /**\n     * Execute a callback within the context of a specific tenant.\n     */\n    public function runAs(string $tenantId, callable $callback): mixed;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Upward**: Registered as a Middleware (`CORE-05`) in the `HUB-08` Gateway.\n- **Downward**: Spoke applications inject `TenancyInterface` to filter queries and scoped storage.\n- **Contract**: Global models must implement a `BelongsToTenant` trait that automatically applies scoping.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Leak Prevention**: A query for \"Users\" while Tenant A is active must return 0 results if all users belong to Tenant B.\n- **Resolution Speed**: Tenant resolution from a host header must take < 0.1ms.\n- **Isolation**: Tenant-specific cache keys (`HUB-02`) must be prefixed with the Tenant ID.\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Major**. Transforms the stack into a multi-tenant platform.\n\n=== HUB-22 ===\n# PHASE HUB-22: Billing & Subscription Abstraction Layer\n\n## Tier\nHub (Shared Services)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Ledger (Billing)\n\n## Description\nA provider-agnostic billing and subscription layer. It abstracts the complexities of Stripe, Paddle, or custom billing engines into a unified API. It manages plans, subscriptions, invoices, and payment methods.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nDepends on `HUB-21` (Tenancy) for billing isolation and `HUB-20` (Vault) for secure API keys.\n\n## Context7 Research\n- **Direct Hub Dependencies**: `HUB-21: Tenancy`, `HUB-20: Vault`, `HUB-06: Audit Log`, `HUB-17: Webhooks`.\n- **Transitive Core Dependencies**: `CORE-19: DBAL`, `CORE-03: Event Dispatcher`.\n- **Abstraction**: Implements the \"Payment Gateway\" pattern.\n\n## Architectural Design\n- **BillingManager**: The entry point for subscription checks and checkout creation.\n- **SubscriptionEngine**: Tracks the state of a tenant's subscription (Active, Trialling, Past Due).\n- **InvoiceManager**: Generates and stores internal invoice records.\n- **WebhookHandler**: Specifically handles billing-related webhooks via `HUB-17`.\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### BillingInterface\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\Hub\\Contracts;\n\ninterface BillingInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Check if a tenant has an active subscription to a specific plan.\n     */\n    public function subscribed(string $tenantId, string $plan): bool;\n\n    /**\n     * Create a checkout session for a specific plan.\n     */\n    public function checkout(string $tenantId, string $plan): string;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Upward**: Consumes `HUB-17` for async payment updates.\n- **Downward**: Spoke applications use `BillingInterface` to guard features and initiate payments.\n- **Contract**: Emits `SubscriptionUpdated` events on `HUB-09` for downstream processing.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Mocking**: Must pass a suite using a \"Mock Billing Driver\" without external network calls.\n- **State Accuracy**: Subscription status must correctly transition from `trialling` to `active` upon a simulated successful payment.\n- **Security**: Credit card data must never touch the Sovereign server (enforced via driver design).\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Minor**. Adds monetization capabilities.\n\n=== HUB-23 ===\n# PHASE HUB-23: Data Export & Reporting Service\n\n## Tier\nHub (Shared Services)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Reporter\n\n## Description\nA service for generating large-scale data exports (CSV, Excel, PDF) and scheduled reports. It coordinates the extraction of data from `CORE-19`, handles background generation via `HUB-10`, and manages delivery via `HUB-12` or `HUB-11`.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nDepends on `HUB-11` (Storage) for output files and `HUB-10` (Queue) for long-running tasks.\n\n## Context7 Research\n- **Direct Hub Dependencies**: `HUB-11: File Storage`, `HUB-10: Queue & Job Dispatcher`, `HUB-12: Notification Service`.\n- **Transitive Core Dependencies**: `CORE-19: DBAL`, `CORE-14: Filesystem`.\n- **Efficiency**: Uses PHP Generators to stream data from DB to file, maintaining a flat memory profile.\n\n## Architectural Design\n- **ExportCoordinator**: Orchestrates the export lifecycle.\n- **DataStreamer**: Efficiently iterates over large datasets from the DBAL.\n- **FormatWriter**: Encapsulates logic for writing CSV or Excel (OpenXML) formats.\n- **ReportScheduler**: Hooks into `HUB-25` (to be defined) for recurring reports.\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### ReporterInterface\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\Hub\\Contracts;\n\ninterface ReporterInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Queue an asynchronous export.\n     */\n    public function queueExport(string $query, string $format, array $options = []): string;\n\n    /**\n     * Get the status or download URL of a queued export.\n     */\n    public function getExportStatus(string $exportId): array;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Upward**: Built on the `HUB-10` Queue system.\n- **Downward**: Spoke applications provide \"Export Blueprints\" (SQL queries + headers) to the Reporter.\n- **Contract**: Notifies the user via `HUB-12` once the file is ready in `HUB-11`.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Memory Consistency**: Exporting 100,000 rows to CSV must not exceed 32MB of RAM.\n- **Format Integrity**: Generated CSVs must be valid (correct quoting/escaping of special characters).\n- **Expiration**: Export files in `HUB-11` must be automatically deleted after 24 hours.\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Minor**. Adds reporting and data mobility features.\n\n=== HUB-24 ===\n# PHASE HUB-24: GraphQL Schema Registry (Pure PHP)\n\n## Tier\nHub (Shared Services)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign GraphQL Registry\n\n## Description\nA pure PHP implementation of a GraphQL schema registry and execution engine. It allows Hub services and Spoke applications to register their own schema fragments (Types, Queries, Mutations) which are then unified into a single, performant API.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nDepends on `HUB-08` (Gateway) for routing and `HUB-04` (Identity) for field-level authorization.\n\n## Context7 Research\n- **Direct Hub Dependencies**: `HUB-08: API Gateway`, `HUB-04: Identity`, `HUB-05: RBAC`.\n- **Transitive Core Dependencies**: `CORE-02: DI Container`, `CORE-06: Router`, `CORE-04: HTTP Message`.\n- **Engine**: Built using `webonyx/graphql-php`. Strictly no Node.js/Apollo.\n- **Patterns**: Schema Stitching (PHP-side), Data Loader (for N+1 prevention).\n\n## Architectural Design\n- **SchemaRegistry**: Collects schema definitions from all registered Hub and Spoke providers.\n- **UnifiedExecutor**: Validates and executes GraphQL queries against the stitched schema.\n- **DirectiveEngine**: Implements PHP-based directives for `@auth`, `@cache`, and `@tenant`.\n- **BatchResolver**: Implements the Data Loader pattern to optimize database queries.\n\n### Schema Registration Example\n```php\n$registry->register('blog', [\n    'type_defs' => 'type Post { id: ID!, title: String! }',\n    'resolvers' => [\n        'Query' => ['post' => fn($root, $args) => $db->find($args['id'])]\n    ]\n]);\n```\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### GraphQLInterface\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\Hub\\Contracts;\n\ninterface GraphQLInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Execute a GraphQL query string.\n     */\n    public function execute(string $query, array $variables = [], mixed $context = null): array;\n\n    /**\n     * Register a new schema fragment.\n     */\n    public function register(string $namespace, array $definition): void;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Upward**: Exposed via a single `/graphql` endpoint in `HUB-08`.\n- **Downward**: Spoke applications provide `SchemaProvider` classes that the Registry discovers during boot.\n- **Contract**: Resolvers must return raw arrays or objects that the engine converts to JSON.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Validation**: Schema stitching must fail if two namespaces attempt to define the same Root Query field.\n- **Security**: Must enforce field-level RBAC (`HUB-05`) via directives.\n- **Performance**: A complex multi-join query must execute in < 20ms (using Data Loaders).\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Minor**. Enables modern, typed data fetching across the stack.\n\n=== HUB-25 ===\n# PHASE HUB-25: Background Scheduler & Cron Management\n\n## Tier\nHub (Shared Services)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Chronos (Scheduler)\n\n## Description\nA centralized scheduler for recurring background tasks. It replaces traditional crontab entries with a PHP-driven fluent interface. It manages task overlaps, execution logs, and provides a unified dashboard for system-wide automation.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nDepends on `HUB-10` (Queue) to dispatch scheduled jobs and `HUB-02` (Cache) for task locking.\n\n## Context7 Research\n- **Direct Hub Dependencies**: `HUB-10: Queue & Job Dispatcher`, `HUB-02: Shared Cache`, `HUB-06: Audit Log`.\n- **Transitive Core Dependencies**: `CORE-13: CLI Engine`, `CORE-19: DBAL`.\n- **Patterns**: Task Scheduler, Cron Expression Parser.\n\n## Architectural Design\n- **ScheduleRegistry**: Holds the list of all recurring tasks and their frequencies.\n- **TaskRunner**: Evaluates which tasks are due and dispatches them to `HUB-10`.\n- **LockManager**: Uses `HUB-02` to ensure a task does not run concurrently on multiple nodes (atomic locks).\n- **HistoryTracker**: Records start time, end time, and output of every execution in `HUB-06`.\n\n### Scheduler Example\n```php\n$schedule->command('cleanup:logs')->dailyAt('00:00')->withoutOverlapping();\n$schedule->job(new DataSyncJob())->everyFiveMinutes();\n```\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### SchedulerInterface\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\Hub\\Contracts;\n\ninterface SchedulerInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Define a recurring CLI command.\n     */\n    public function command(string $signature): TaskInterface;\n\n    /**\n     * Define a recurring background job.\n     */\n    public function job(object $job): TaskInterface;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Upward**: Requires a single system-level cron entry to run the `s-cli schedule:run` command every minute.\n- **Downward**: Spoke applications register tasks in their `ServiceProvider` (`CORE-17`).\n- **Contract**: Tasks are dispatched as standard `HUB-10` jobs.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Overlap Prevention**: Must verify that a second instance of a \"withoutOverlapping\" task does not start if the first is still running.\n- **Precision**: Tasks scheduled for \"every minute\" must trigger within 1 second of the clock minute.\n- **Log Integrity**: Failed tasks must be flagged in the history registry with their exception trace.\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Minor**. Both have been corrected; this section is now the single place the\npolicy is stated so it doesn't drift again:\n\n**Every entity primary/foreign-key identifier in the Sovereign Stack — tenant, user, audit record,\ntenant-config override row, or otherwise — is a ULID** (26-character, Crockford Base32,\nlexicographically sortable), not a database auto-increment integer and not a UUID. ULID is chosen over\nUUID specifically because its sortability keeps index locality reasonable for high-write tables like\n`HUB-06`'s audit log. The one documented exception: surrogate/internal-only primary keys with no\nexternal reference or cross-service meaning (e.g., `HUB-01.md`'s `hub_config_overrides.id`, which is\nnever referenced outside that single table) may remain `BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT` — the distinction is\nwhether an ID is ever passed between services or exposed to a client (ULID) versus purely local to one\ntable's own row identity (integer is fine).\n\nAny blueprint introducing a new entity type must state which of the two categories its primary key\nfalls into, rather than picking a type ad hoc. **Two real inconsistencies, self-introduced:**
1. `HUB-06.md`'s audit-record schema typed `tenant_id`/`user_id` as `"int"` while `HUB-01.md` and `HUB-21.md` established `tenant_id` as a `CHAR(26)` ULID — three different ID schemes for one concept, inside the delivery meant to fix exactly this pattern. Fixed by standardizing on ULID and adding both a `HUB-21` note and a stack-wide `01_MASTER_INDEX.md` §10 policy (ULID for anything cross-service, integer only for purely-local surrogate keys) so it's stated once instead of restated ad hoc per file.
2. A leftover sentence in `HUB-21.md` from an earlier edit that failed silently (my `str_replace` call dropped a required parameter and I moved on without noticing) still attributed the tenant ID format to `HUB-04`. Also fixed.

Both are corrected now, and I called it out in the master index rather than fixing it quietly, since that's the standard the critique itself demands.

For the record, that's the state of the batch so far: `CORE-01`, `CORE-02`, `HUB-01`–`HUB-21`, `BRIDGE-01`, `ISPOKE-01`, `ESPOKE-01`, `DEPLOY-01`. Still open: `HUB-22`–`HUB-30`, then Spokes and the rest of Deploy. Want me to keep going with `HUB-22`–`30` next, or pause here so you can review what's landed before I add more surface area?

1	## 00_CRITIQUE.md
     2	
     3	# DGLab Blueprint System — Critique & Findings
     4	
     5	**Scope of review:** `docs/architecture/origin/**`, `docs/blueprints/**`, `docs/evaluation/**`,
     6	`docs/hub-taxonomy/**`, `docs/internal-spokes/**`, `packages/**`, `orchestrator/**`, `render.yaml`,
     7	`SESSION_STATE.md`, plus supporting cross-checks against real code in the repo.
     8	
     9	Every finding below is traceable to specific files/lines in `DGCodeIdeas/DGLab` (`main` branch, as of
    10	this review). This is a technical audit, not a restatement of the repo's own self-assessment — several
    11	findings directly contradict the repo's own `docs/evaluation/` scores.
    12	
    13	---
    14	
    15	## Finding 1 — Two incompatible architectures share the "Sovereign Stack / Hub-and-Spoke" name
    16	
    17	The repository contains **two mutually exclusive systems**, both calling themselves "Sovereign Stack"
    18	and both calling themselves "Hub-and-Spoke," with no document anywhere stating that one supersedes
    19	the other.
    20	
    21	**Vision A — the monolith rebuild** (`docs/architecture/origin/HUB_AND_SPOKE.md`,
    22	`STRATEGIC_OVERVIEW.md`, `DETAILED_SYSTEM_ANALYSIS.md`, `ComponentBlueprints/**`, `PhasedBlueprints/**`,
    23	`Strategic/DGLAB_STRATEGIC_BLUEPRINT.md`, `Strategic/STRATEGIC_BLUEPRINT.md`,
    24	`Sovereign_Stack_Blueprint/**`):
    25	- "Hub" = **CMS Studio**, a single monolithic PHP application, the *only* service exposed to the
    26	  internet.
    27	- "Spokes" = plain PHP classes living in `app/Spokes/`, resolved in-process
    28	  (`Application::getInstance()->get(MangaScriptSpoke::class)`), with **zero independent routing**.
    29	- Front end = SuperPHP + "Superpowers" SPA (server-side DOM morphing), Nexus (Swoole WebSockets),
    30	  MangaScript (AI novel→manga), matching the actual code under `Legacy/app/**`.
    31	
    32	**Vision B — the polyrepo rebuild** (`docs/blueprints/Core|Hub|Spoke|Bridge|Deploy/**`,
    33	`docs/hub-taxonomy/**`, `docs/internal-spokes/**`, `docs/external-spokes/**`, `orchestrator/**`,
    34	`packages/core/**`):
    35	- "Hub" = a *tier* of ~30 independent shared-service repos (config, cache, auth, queue…).
    36	- "Spokes" = separate Internal/External **applications**, each a distinct deployable, isolated by a
    37	  mandatory `BRIDGE-01` security boundary.
    38	- `CORE-01` is literally a **Polyrepo Orchestrator** — i.e., the system explicitly assumes multiple
    39	  git repositories, the opposite of Vision A's single codebase.
    40	
    41	These aren't variations on a theme — Vision A is a modular monolith, Vision B is a distributed,
    42	tier-isolated system with a completely different security model, deployment topology, and dependency
    43	direction. A developer or agent reading `docs/architecture/origin/HUB_AND_SPOKE.md` first will build
    44	the wrong mental model for everything under `docs/blueprints/`, and vice versa. Nothing in either tree
    45	cross-references the other or explains which one is current. (Circumstantial evidence, e.g. the
    46	`orchestrator/` and `packages/core/*` code and this project's own recent working history, indicates
    47	Vision B is the active effort — but that is inference from the filesystem, not documentation.)
    48	
    49	## Finding 2 — The evaluation layer scored a version of Core that no longer exists
    50	
    51	`docs/evaluation/BLUEPRINT_RANKINGS.md` and `EVALUATION_SUMMARY.md` assign scores and descriptions to
    52	`CORE-01`…`CORE-20` that **do not match a single one of the current files** in `docs/blueprints/Core/`:
    53	
    54	| ID | Evaluation doc claims | Actual current file content |
    55	|----|------------------------|------------------------------|
    56	| CORE-01 | "Bootstrapper & Kernel" (94/100) | **Polyrepo Orchestrator ("The Loom")** |
    57	| CORE-02 | "Lifecycle Hooks" (89/100) | **Dependency Injection Container** |
    58	| CORE-03 | "Service Container" (92/100) | **PSR-14 Event Dispatcher** |
    59	| CORE-04 | "Encryption Primitives" (89/100) | **PSR-7 HTTP Message & Factory** |
    60	| CORE-05 | "Router & Dispatch" (91/100) | **PSR-15 Middleware & Request Handler** |
    61	| CORE-06 | "Request/Response" (89/100) | **Attribute-Based Router** |
    62	| CORE-07 | "Middleware Pipeline" (90/100) | **SuperPHP Lexer** |
    63	| CORE-08 | "Filesystem Abstraction" (89/100) | **Global Error & Exception Handler** |
    64	| CORE-09 | "Error Handling" (91/100) | **PSR-3 Logging Service** |
    65	| CORE-11 | "ORM & Query Builder" (88/100) | **SuperPHP Parser** |
    66	| CORE-14 | "Caching Layer" (85/100) | **Filesystem Abstraction** |
    67	| CORE-15 | "Validation Engine" (86/100) | **Cache Abstraction (PSR-6/16)** |
    68	| CORE-16 | "Logging & Observability" (84/100) | **Binary Encryption Envelope** |
    69	| CORE-17 | "Testing Framework" (85/100) | **Service Provider System** |
    70	| CORE-18 | "Event System" (83/100) | **Core Kernel & Lifecycle ("The Sovereign Kernel")** |
    71	| CORE-19 | "Service Locator" (82/100) | **Database Abstraction Layer** |
    72	
    73	Every score, every "why it's critical" rationale, and the recommended "Implementation Sequence"
    74	(`CORE-01 → CORE-02 → CORE-03 → CORE-05 → CORE-06 → CORE-07...`) in the ranking doc is built on the
    75	**old** numbering, where the kernel was `CORE-01`. In the **current** numbering the kernel is
    76	`CORE-18` — second-to-last in the tier. Taken literally, the evaluation's own recommended sequence
    77	would have you build the polyrepo release-automation tool and a router *before* the kernel that boots
    78	the application at all. The 87/100 "overall quality score" is not a reflection of what's actually in
    79	`docs/blueprints/` today; it's a snapshot of an earlier renumbering that was never reconciled.
    80	
    81	## Finding 3 — Cross-references inside the *current* canonical set are already wrong
    82	
    83	This isn't only inherited drift from an old numbering — the "approved" blueprints contradict each
    84	other today:
    85	
    86	- `docs/blueprints/Spoke/Bridge/BRIDGE-01.md` lists a Core dependency as
    87	  `CORE-09: Cryptography & Hashing (Payload Verification)`.
    88	- The actual `docs/blueprints/Core/CORE-09.md` is titled **"PSR-3 Logging Service / Structured Logging
    89	  Engine"** — it has nothing to do with cryptography.
    90	- The component that *is* cryptography is `CORE-16` ("Binary Encryption Envelope / Cryptographic
    91	  Foundation").
    92	
    93	So the single most security-critical document in the whole system (BRIDGE-01, self-rated 96/100,
    94	"single most important architectural innovation") cites the wrong upstream dependency for its own
    95	payload-verification logic. By contrast, cross-references to Hub-tier IDs from `ISPOKE-01`,
    96	`ESPOKE-01`, and `BRIDGE-01` (e.g., `HUB-04` Identity, `HUB-06` Audit, `HUB-15` Health,
    97	`HUB-26` UI library) were checked and **do** match `docs/hub-taxonomy/hub-blueprint-taxonomy.md` and
    98	the actual Hub blueprint titles — so the drift is concentrated in the Core tier's internal renumbering,
    99	not a general symptom across every tier.
   100	
   101	## Finding 4 — "Approved" isn't a proxy for "more substantive"
   102	
   103	Compare `docs/blueprints/disapproved/CORE-01.md` (rejected) with `docs/blueprints/Core/CORE-01.md`
   104	(approved):
   105	
   106	- **Disapproved CORE-01** ("Foundational Bootstrapper & Kernel"): defines a `KernelInterface`, a typed
   107	  `Environment` enum, a complete `ErrorHandler` class with real PHP 8 code (`set_error_handler`,
   108	  `set_exception_handler`, `register_shutdown_function`), a boot-sequence **sequence diagram**, named
   109	  performance budgets tied to a specific mechanism (OPcache preload), and 100%-path unit test criteria.
   110	  ~7.6 KB.
   111	- **Approved CORE-01** ("Polyrepo Orchestrator"): five short prose sections, one Mermaid flowchart,
   112	  zero code, zero interfaces. ~2.2 KB.
   113	
   114	The disapproval note for the entire 72-file `disapproved/` folder is a single generic sentence —
   115	see Finding 12 — so there's no record of *why* the more code-complete, more rigorous document was the
   116	one rejected. Whatever the actual reason, "disapproved" is being used here as a synonym for
   117	"doesn't match the current template," not "lower engineering quality," and the evaluation layer treats
   118	the 81 "approved" docs as uniformly higher quality than the 72 rejected ones without ever comparing them
   119	side by side.
   120	
   121	## Finding 5 — A byte-for-byte duplicate masquerading as a mobile-optimized variant
   122	
   123	`docs/architecture/origin/Sovereign_Stack_Blueprint/SOVEREIGN_STACK_MASTER.md` and
   124	`docs/architecture/origin/Mobile_Optimized/SOVEREIGN_STACK_MASTER.md` are **identical**
   125	(`md5sum` match, `diff` produces zero lines) — both 126,420 bytes. The `Mobile_Optimized/` directory
   126	name promises a variant tuned for mobile reading/consumption; it contains none. This is either an
   127	abandoned task or a copy left in place by mistake, but either way it doubles the maintenance surface
   128	of a 126 KB document with zero benefit, and it will silently drift out of sync the next time only one
   129	copy gets edited.
   130	
   131	## Finding 6 — Sibling documents in the same folder contradict each other's completion claims
   132	
   133	Within `docs/architecture/origin/PhasedBlueprints/`:
   134	- `README.md`'s status table marks **Nexus** as `✅ COMPLETED (CORE)`.
   135	- `ANALYSIS_REPORT.md`, sitting in the same directory, states **Nexus is 40% complete** ("L3: Building
   136	  … Phases 1-2 Completed").
   137	
   138	Within the wider `docs/architecture/origin/ComponentBlueprints/` tree:
   139	- `README.md` marks **AdminPanel** as `✅ COMPLETED (LEGACY)`.
	< truncated lines 140-966 >
   967	- 100% branch coverage on `make()`, `autowire()`, and `compile()` — these are the three methods every
   968	  downstream tier depends on transitively; regressions here are systemic, not local.
   969	- `phpstan.neon` at the level already configured in `packages/core/container/phpstan.neon` must pass
   970	  with zero baseline-ignored errors introduced by this implementation.
   971	- No dependency added beyond `psr/container` without a corresponding update to
   972	  `01_MASTER_INDEX.md` (this container is meant to stay dependency-free by design — that's part of
   973	  its contract with the rest of the Core tier).
   974	
   975	## SemVer Impact
   976	**Major** for the package itself (`sovereign-stack/core-container` `1.0.0` — first real release);
   977	this is also the change that unblocks Hub-tier work from "documented but blocked" to "documented and
   978	buildable," so it should be treated as unblocking the *program's* critical path, not just shipping one
   979	package.
   980	
   981	
   982	---
   983	
   984	## HUB-01.md
   985	
   986	# PHASE HUB-01: Global Configuration & Feature Flags
   987	
   988	## Tier
   989	Hub (Shared Services)
   990	
   991	## Resolves
   992	`00_CRITIQUE.md` Finding 8 (this blueprint's dependency on `CORE-02` is now explicitly marked
   993	**blocked** rather than silently assumed available) and Finding 10 (verification criteria now state a
   994	method, not just a number).
   995	
   996	## Component Name
   997	Sovereign Hub Config & Flags
   998	
   999	## Description
  1000	A global configuration management service that extends `CORE-10` (Config & Environment Loader) to
  1001	support multi-tenant configuration overrides, dynamic feature flags, and remote settings, so that Hub
  1002	and Spoke applications can toggle functionality without redeploying.
  1003	
  1004	## Build Status
  1005	🔴 **Blocked.** Depends on `CORE-02` (DI Container), which per `01_MASTER_INDEX.md` §2 has zero
  1006	implementation. Depends on `CORE-10` (Config & Environment Loader), also not yet started. Do not begin
  1007	implementation work on this blueprint until both land — see the revised sequence in
  1008	`01_MASTER_INDEX.md` §5.
  1009	
  1010	## Dependency Status
  1011	- **Upward:** `CORE-10` (Config & Env Loader), `CORE-02` (DI Container), `CORE-19` (DBAL — for
  1012	  tenant-specific dynamic overrides persisted to a database rather than static files).
  1013	- **Downward:** every subsequent Hub service and all Spokes consume `GlobalConfigInterface`.
  1014	
  1015	## Sequencing Rationale
  1016	First Hub-tier phase because every later Hub service (Identity, Cache, Asset Pipeline) needs a single,
  1017	consistent way to read shared settings and evaluate feature toggles.
  1018	
  1019	## Architectural Design
  1020	
  1021	- **HubConfigRegistry** — merges static defaults (from `CORE-10`) with tenant-specific overrides
  1022	  (persisted via `CORE-19`). Merge direction is strict: tenant overrides may only *add or replace* keys
  1023	  explicitly present in their own override table; a tenant override can never introduce a key absent
  1024	  from the global default schema (prevents silent, unvalidated schema drift per tenant).
  1025	- **FeatureFlagManager** — evaluates toggle states against a `Context` (user, tenant, environment,
  1026	  and optionally a rollout-percentage bucket computed from a stable hash of the context's identifier,
  1027	  so a given user consistently lands on the same side of a percentage rollout across requests).
  1028	- **FlagEvaluator** — the rule engine behind `FeatureFlagManager`; supports boolean toggles,
  1029	  percentage rollouts, and allow/deny lists by tenant or user ID.
  1030	
  1031	### Interface Contracts
  1032	
  1033	```php
  1034	namespace SovereignStack\Hub\Config\Contracts;
  1035	
  1036	interface GlobalConfigInterface
  1037	{
  1038	    /** Get a configuration value with tenant-aware fallback to the global default. */
  1039	    public function get(string $key, mixed $default = null, ?string $tenantId = null): mixed;
  1040	
  1041	    /** Check whether a feature flag is active for the current resolved context. */
  1042	    public function feature(string $flag): bool;
  1043	}
  1044	
  1045	interface FeatureManagerInterface
  1046	{
  1047	    public function isEnabled(string $flag, ?Context $context = null): bool;
  1048	
  1049	    /** For multi-variant flags (e.g., A/B/C), returns the variant key, not just on/off. */
  1050	    public function getVariant(string $flag, ?Context $context = null): string;
  1051	}
  1052	
  1053	/** Immutable evaluation context — construct once per request. */
  1054	final class Context
  1055	{
  1056	    public function __construct(
  1057	        public readonly ?string $userId = null,
  1058	        public readonly ?string $tenantId = null,
  1059	        public readonly string $environment = 'production',
  1060	    ) {}
  1061	}
  1062	```
  1063	
  1064	### Tenant Override Data Model
  1065	
  1066	```
  1067	hub_config_overrides
  1068	  id            BIGINT PK
  1069	  tenant_id     CHAR(26)   -- ULID, matches HUB-04 tenant identifier format
  1070	  config_key    VARCHAR(191)
  1071	  config_value  JSON
  1072	  updated_at    TIMESTAMP
  1073	  UNIQUE (tenant_id, config_key)
  1074	```
  1075	
  1076	### Merge Logic Guarantee
  1077	A tenant override row may only be applied if `config_key` already exists in the static default
  1078	configuration schema loaded from `CORE-10`. `HubConfigRegistry::get()` must validate this at read time
  1079	(or, preferably, `CompilerPassInterface`-style validation at boot, rejecting invalid override rows
  1080	loudly rather than silently ignoring them) — this is the concrete mechanism behind the "tenant
  1081	overrides must never leak into the global pool" requirement below.
  1082	
  1083	## Integration Strategy
  1084	- **Upward:** consumes `CORE-10` for static defaults, `CORE-19` for dynamic tenant overrides,
  1085	  `CORE-02` to be constructed and injected as a singleton.
  1086	- **Downward:** injected as a singleton into every Hub and Spoke service provider via the container's
  1087	  `singleton()` binding (see `CORE-02`).
  1088	
  1089	## Benchmark & Verification Methodology
  1090	| Target | Method |
  1091	|---|---|
  1092	| Tenant overrides never leak into the global default pool | Integration test: write an override for tenant A, then assert `get($key, tenantId: 'B')` and `get($key, tenantId: null)` both still return the unmodified default. |
  1093	| A dynamic flag change (written via `CORE-19`) is observable within a bounded, documented window | Integration test measuring wall-clock between a direct DB write and the next `feature()` call observing the new value, across a cache-invalidation cycle if `HUB-02` caching is in front of this service. State the actual measured number here once `HUB-02` is implemented — do not assert "within 1 second" without running this test, per Governance Rule 2. |
  1094	| Percentage rollout is stable per user across repeated evaluations | Unit test: call `getVariant()` 100 times for the same `Context.userId` and assert identical results every time (verifies the hash-bucket approach is deterministic, not re-randomized per call). |
  1095	
  1096	## CI Verification Criteria
  1097	- Merge logic: automated test asserting cross-tenant isolation (see above) — this is a security
  1098	  property, not just a correctness one, and should be treated with the same rigor as `BRIDGE-01`'s
  1099	  isolation tests.
  1100	- Consistency: cache-invalidation window is measured and documented, not asserted from memory.
  1101	- No override may reference a `config_key` absent from the `CORE-10` default schema (validated at
  1102	  write time, not just read time, to fail fast).
  1103	
  1104	## SemVer Impact
  1105	**Minor.** Extends `CORE-10`'s configuration surface without changing its contract.

1	# PHASE ISPOKE-08: Workflow and Task Management System
     2	
     3	## Tier
     4	Internal Spoke (Staff-only Application)
     5	
     6	## Component Name
     7	Sovereign Flow
     8	
     9	## Description
    10	A state-machine driven workflow engine for managing complex internal processes (e.g., staff onboarding, content approval chains, incident response). It provides a UI for task assignment, deadline tracking, and automated transitions based on business rules.
    11	
    12	## Sequencing Rationale
    13	Relies on ISPOKE-07 for task notifications. This system becomes the foundational orchestration layer for all human-in-the-loop processes in the stack.
    14	
    15	## Context7 Research
    16	### Direct Hub Dependencies
    17	- `HUB-05: RBAC & Permission Engine`
    18	- `HUB-10: Task Queue & Scheduled Jobs`
    19	- `HUB-12: Event-driven Messaging & Pub/Sub`
    20	- `HUB-26: Shared UI Component Library`
    21	- `HUB-08: API Gateway`
    22	- `HUB-15: Health Check & Service Discovery`
    23	
    24	### Transitive Core Dependencies
    25	- `CORE-18: Core Kernel & Lifecycle`
    26	- `CORE-19: DBAL & Migrations`
    27	- `CORE-02: DI Container`
    28	- `CORE-11: SuperPHP Parser`
    29	- `CORE-12: SuperPHP Compiler`
    30	
    31	## Architectural Design
    32	- **StateMachine**: Defines valid states (e.g., Pending, In Progress, Review, Done) and transitions.
    33	- **TaskRegistry**: Tracks active assignments, priorities, and deadlines.
    34	- **RuleEvaluator**: Automatically triggers transitions based on data conditions or external events.
    35	- **FlowBuilder**: A low-code UI for defining new workflow definitions (stored as Hub Config).
    36	
    37	### State Transition Diagram
    38	```mermaid
    39	stateDiagram-v2
    40	    [*] --> Pending
    41	    Pending --> InProgress: Assign
    42	    InProgress --> Review: Submit
    43	    Review --> Done: Approve
    44	    Review --> InProgress: Reject
    45	    InProgress --> [*]: Cancel
    46	```
    47	
    48	## Interface Contracts
    49	
    50	### WorkflowEngineInterface
    51	```php
    52	namespace Sovereign\Internal\Flow\Contracts;
    53	
    54	interface WorkflowEngineInterface
    55	{
    56	    /**
    57	     * Start a new workflow instance for a given entity.
    58	     */
    59	    public function start(string $flowType, string $entityId, array $context = []): string;
    60	
    61	    /**
    62	     * Transition a task to the next state.
    63	     */
    64	    public function transition(string $taskId, string $transition, string $staffId): bool;
    65	}
    66	```
    67	
    68	## Integration Strategy
    69	- **Bootstrapping**: Loads workflow definitions from `HUB-01` and registers listeners on `HUB-12`.
    70	- **UI**: Provides specialized "Kanban" and "List" view components via `HUB-26`.
    71	- **Notifications**: Automatically triggers `ISPOKE-07` Relay notifications on task assignment or state change.
    72	- **Auditing**: Every transition is logged in `HUB-06` via the Hub API Gateway.
    73	- **Health**: Reports stalled workflows (past deadline) to `HUB-15`.
    74	
    75	## CI Verification Criteria
    76	- **Transition Integrity**: Invalid state transitions (e.g., Pending -> Done) must be blocked at the interface level.
    77	- **Atomic Updates**: State changes and transition logging must be transactional.
    78	- **Concurrency**: Must handle 100 simultaneous transition requests on the same workflow type without deadlocks.
    79	
    80	## SemVer Impact
    81	**Major**. Introduces a critical coordination layer for all staff-driven operations.

     6	## Component Name

     8

    10	A collaborative documentation and knowledge management platform for staff. It serves as the "Sovereign Manual," containing standard operating procedures (SOPs), technical documentation, and organizational policies. It features Markdown editing, version history, and full-text search.
    11	
    12	## Sequencing Rationale

    14

    30	- `CORE-12: SuperPHP Compiler`
    31	- `CORE-06: Router`
    32

    34	- **DocumentManager**: Handles CRUD operations for Markdown documents and their metadata.
    35	- **VersionControl**: Tracks document revisions and allows for side-by-side diffing and rollback.

    38

    41	graph TD

    44	    DM --> VC[Version Control]
    45	    DM --> S[HUB-13: Search Index]
    46	    DM --> M[HUB-14: Media Library]

    48	```
    49	
    50	## Interface Contracts
    51	
    52	### KnowledgeBaseInterface
    53	```php
    54	namespace Sovereign\Internal\Codex\Contracts;
    55	
    56	interface KnowledgeBaseInterface
    57	{
    58	    /**
    59	     * Retrieve a document by slug, including version metadata.
    60	     */
    61	    public function getDocument(string $slug, ?int $version = null): array;
    62	
    63	    /**
    64	     * Create or update a document.
    65	     */
    66	    public function saveDocument(string $slug, string $content, string $staffId, string $summary): bool;
    67	}
    68	```
    69	
    70	## Integration Strategy
    71	- **Bootstrapping**: Initialized via `CORE-18`; verifies search availability via `HUB-15`.
    72	- **Authoring**: Uses a reactive Markdown editor built with `HUB-26` components.
    73	- **Indexing**: Automatically pushes document updates to `HUB-13` via a background job (`HUB-11`).
    74	- **Permissions**: Respects "Departmental" access levels defined in `HUB-05`.
    75	- **Health**: Reports search latency and indexing backlog to `HUB-15`.

Health Check & Service Discovery (Latency Monitoring)`\n\n### Transitive Core Dependencies\n- `CORE-14: Filesystem Abstraction (IO)`\n- `CORE-18: Core Kernel & Lifecycle (Runtime)`\n- `CORE-06: Router on-the-fly resizing and optimization.\n- **CacheLayer**: Implements high-performance edge-caching patterns using `HUB-02`.\n- **SecurityProxy**: Validates signatures for requested assets to prevent \"image resizing\" DoS attacks.\n- **PrismRouter**: Maps public URLs to Bridge-requested internal media IDs.\n\n### Asset Delivery Flow\n```mermaid\ngraph TD\n    U[End User] --> G[HUB-08: Gateway]\n    G --> P[ESPOKE-08: Prism]\n    P --> S{Signed?}\n    S -- No --> D[403 Forbidden]\n    S -- Yes --> C{In Cache?}\n    C -- Yes --> R[Serve from HUB-02]\n    C -- No --> B[BRIDGE-01: Bridge]\n    B --> I[Internal Media Store]\n    I --> B\n    B --> T[Transformation Engine]\n    T --> L[Cache in HUB-02]\n    L --> R\n```\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### MediaDeliveryBridgeContract\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\External\\Prism\\Contracts;\n\nuse Sovereign\\Bridge\\Contracts\\BoundaryContractInterface;\n\n/**\n * Specifically governs media asset retrieval across the boundary.\n */\ninterface MediaDeliveryBridgeContract extends BoundaryContractInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Retrieve a public-safe stream or path for a media asset.\n     */\n    public function getPublicAsset(string $assetId): array;\n\n    /**\n     * Check if a specific media asset is marked as \"Public\" in the internal registry.\n     */\n    public function isPubliclyAccessible(string $assetId): bool;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Bridge Compliance**: Interacts with internal storage ONLY through `MediaDeliveryBridgeContract`. No direct filesystem access to internal directories.\n- **Signed URLs**: All transformation parameters (width, height, quality) must be signed using a secret from `CORE-09` to prevent abuse.\n- **Format Negotiation**: Automatically detects browser support (e.g., WebP, Avif) and serves the most efficient format.\n- **SuperPHP Integration**: Provides a `<s:ui:image />` component in `HUB-26` that automatically generates optimized Prism URLs.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Optimization Ratio**: Transformed images must be at least 30% smaller than originals on average.\n- **Signature Security**: Requesting a transformation with an invalid signature MUST return a `403 Forbidden` in < 2ms.\n- **Cache Efficiency**: > 90% cache hit rate for repeated asset requests in simulated load tests.\n- **Bridge Leak Test**: Automated tests must verify that an \"Internal-only\" asset ID returns a `404 Not Found` when requested via Prism.\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Minor**. Enhances delivery performance and security for the public web surface.\n\n=== ESPOKE-09 ===\n# PHASE ESPOKE-09: E-Commerce and Checkout Portal\n\n## Tier\nExternal Spoke (Public-facing Application)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Market (Checkout)\n\n## Description\nA secure, high-conversion e-commerce and checkout application. It manages shopping carts, calculates taxes/shipping via the Bridge, and coordinates payment processing through an abstraction layer. It consumes exclusively from `HUB-26` for its UI and honors the `BRIDGE-01` boundary for all transaction processing.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nDepends on ESPOKE-08 (Prism) for product imagery and ESPOKE-01 (Canvas) for the shopping experience integration. Precedes ESPOKE-10 (Subscription) as the base checkout logic is established here.\n\n## Context7 Research\n### Direct Hub Dependencies\n- `HUB-26: Shared UI Component Library (Checkout Primitives)`\n- `HUB-04: Global Identity & Authentication (Customer Auth)`\n- `HUB-08: API Gateway & Public Surface (Secure Routing)`\n- `HUB-06: Audit Log & Activity Tracker (Transaction Logging)`\n- `HUB-02: Distributed Cache (Cart Storage)`\n\n### Transitive Core Dependencies\n- `CORE-09: Cryptography & Hashing (PCI Compliance/Signing)`\n- `CORE-18: Core Kernel & Lifecycle (Checkout State Machine)`\n- `CORE-11: SuperPHP Parser (Reactive UI)`\n- `CORE-02: DI Container (Payment Drivers)`\n\n## Architectural Design\n- **CartManager**: A reactive, cache-backed state manager for user shopping sessions.\n- **PaymentAbstractionLayer**: A unified interface for interacting with external providers (e.g., Stripe, PayPal). No direct SDK coupling.\n- **OrderWorkflowEngine**: Orchestrates the transition from \"Cart\" to \"Pending\" to \"Complete\" states.\n- **CheckoutPresenter**: Handles the multi-step checkout UI using `HUB-26` components.\n\n### Checkout Flow Diagram\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant C as Customer\n    participant M as ESPOKE-09 (Market)\n    participant B as BRIDGE-01 (Bridge)\n    participant P as Payment Provider (Stripe)\n    participant I as Internal Spoke (ISPOKE-05: Ledger)\n\n    C->>M: Initiate Checkout\n    M->>B: Validate Cart (CommerceCheckoutBridgeContract)\n    B-->>M: Public DTO (Prices, Taxes)\n    M->>P: Create Payment Intent (via Abstraction)\n    P-->>M: Intent Token\n    C->>M: Submit Payment\n    M->>P: Confirm Payment\n    P-->>M: Success\n    M->>B: Finalize Order (CommerceCheckoutBridgeContract)\n    B->>I: Record Transaction\n    B-->>M: Order Confirmation DTO\n    M-->>C: Receipt Page\n```\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### CommerceCheckoutBridgeContract\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\External\\Market\\Contracts;\n\nuse Sovereign\\Bridge\\Contracts\\BoundaryContractInterface;\n\n/**\n * Specifically governs the checkout boundary crossing.\n */\ninterface CommerceCheckoutBridgeContract extends BoundaryContractInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Validate a cart and return a public-safe pricing breakdown.\n     */\n    public function validateCart(array $items, string $promoCode = null): array;\n\n    /**\n     * Finalize an order after successful payment.\n     */\n    public function finalizeOrder(string $paymentToken, array $customerDetails): array;\n}\n```\n\n### PaymentProviderInterface\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\External\\Market\\Contracts;\n\n/**\n * Abstraction to prevent coupling to specific payment SDKs.\n */\ninterface PaymentProviderInterface\n{\n    public function createIntent(float $amount, string $currency): string;\n    public function capturePayment(string $intentId): bool;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Bridge Compliance**: Never touches internal order or inventory tables directly. Uses `CommerceCheckoutBridgeContract` for all logic verification.\n- **UI Consistency**: Strictly uses `HUB-26` checkout components (forms, summary cards, progress bars).\n- **Security**: No raw credit card data ever touches the Sovereign Stack. All interactions use provider-issued tokens.\n- **Audit**: Every checkout attempt and payment response is logged to `HUB-06` for forensic reconciliation.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Zero-Coupling Check**: Static analysis must confirm that no Stripe or PayPal namespace is used outside of a `Sovereign\\External\\Market\\Drivers` namespace.\n- **Transaction Integrity**: Simulated checkout failures (e.g., network timeout after payment) must result in a consistent \"Recoverable\" state in the Bridge.\n- **Performance**: Checkout UI interactivity (SuperPHP reactivity) must maintain 60FPS on mobile devices.\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Major**. Establishes the revenue-generating engine of the platform.\n\n=== ESPOKE-10 ===\n# PHASE ESPOKE-10: Public Subscription and Billing Portal\n\n## Tier\nExternal Spoke (Public-facing Application)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Pulse (Billing)\n\n## Description\nA dedicated portal for customers to manage their subscriptions, view invoices, and update billing methods. It provides a \"Self-Service Billing\" experience, interacting with the Internal Spoke sub-tier via the Bridge to ensure that sensitive financial and plan data remains protected.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nDepends on ESPOKE-09 for shared payment abstraction logic and ESPOKE-03 (Account Portal) for user context.\n\n## Context7 Research\n### Direct Hub Dependencies\n- `HUB-04: Global Identity & Authentication (Customer Session)`\n- `HUB-26: Shared UI Component Library (Billing UI)`\n- `HUB-08: API Gateway & Public Surface (Protected Access)`\n- `HUB-15: Health Check & Service Discovery (API Latency)`\n\n### Transitive Core Dependencies\n- `CORE-09: Cryptography & Hashing (Sensitive Token Handling)`\n- `CORE-18: Core Kernel & Lifecycle (Subscription Logic)`\n- `CORE-04: HTTP Message (File Downloads - Invoices)`\n- `CORE-12: SuperPHP Compiler (Dynamic UI Generation)`\n\n## Architectural Design\n- **SubscriptionManager**: Manages plan transitions (upgrades/downgrades) and lifecycle states.\n- **InvoiceEngine**: Fetches public-safe invoice data and generates downloadable PDFs via the Bridge.\n- **PaymentMethodVault**: A secure interface for managing saved payment tokens (never raw cards).\n- **PulseUI**: A customer-facing dashboard built with `HUB-26` components.\n\n### Subscription Management Flow\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant C as Customer\n    participant P as ESPOKE-10 (Pulse)\n    participant B as BRIDGE-01 (Bridge)\n    participant I as Internal Spoke (ISPOKE-05: Ledger)\n\n    C->>P: View Billing Dashboard\n    P->>B: Get Account Summary (SubscriptionBillingBridgeContract)\n    B->>I: Fetch Subscription & History\n    I-->>B: Internal Financial Data\n    B->>B: Transform to Public DTO\n    B-->>P: Public-Safe Billing Data\n    P-->>C: Render Dashboard\n    C->>P: Download Invoice\n    P->>B: Request Invoice PDF\n    B-->>P: PDF Stream\n    P-->>C: File Download\n```\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### SubscriptionBillingBridgeContract\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\External\\Pulse\\Contracts;\n\nuse Sovereign\\Bridge\\Contracts\\BoundaryContractInterface;\n\n/**\n * Specifically governs subscription and billing boundary crossing.\n */\ninterface SubscriptionBillingBridgeContract extends BoundaryContractInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Retrieve a summary of the customer's current plans and status.\n     */\n    public function getSubscriptionSummary(string $customerId): array;\n\n    /**\n     * Retrieve a list of invoices for the customer.\n     */\n    public function getInvoices(string $customerId): array;\n\n    /**\n     * Request a plan change.\n     */\n    public function changePlan(string $customerId, string $newPlanId): array;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Bridge Compliance**: Sensitive financial records are never exposed. The Bridge only passes DTOs containing \"safe\" metadata (last 4 digits, expiry, plan names, amounts).\n- **Auth Guard**: Every request to Pulse must be verified against `HUB-04` and `HUB-05` to ensure users only access their own billing data.\n- **Payment Abstraction**: Shares the `PaymentProviderInterface` defined in ESPOKE-09 to handle recurring payment method updates.\n- **PDF Delivery**: Invoices are streamed via the Bridge to ensure they are never stored in a publicly accessible storage bucket.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Privacy Enforcement**: Automated tests must attempt to fetch an invoice for \"Customer A\" using \"Customer B's\" session and verify it returns `403 Forbidden`.\n- **Plan Transition Integrity**: Downgrades must trigger appropriate \"Entitlement Change\" events in the Bridge.\n- **UI Performance**: The billing dashboard must load and become interactive in < 1.5s on 4G connections.\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Major**. Completes the customer lifecycle management for the platform.\n\n=== ESPOKE-11 ===\n# PHASE ESPOKE-11: Customer Self-Service and Support Centre\n\n## Tier\nExternal Spoke (Public-facing Application)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Beacon (Support)\n\n## Description\nA unified self-service portal and support ticket management system. It provides a public-facing interface for customers to find answers (via the public knowledge base) and interact with support staff (via tickets) without ever having direct access to internal staff-only support tools.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nDepends on ESPOKE-01 for page rendering patterns and ESPOKE-03 for customer context. Ticket Creation\n    I-->>G: Internal ID\n    G-->>B: Public Ticket DTO\n    B-->>C: Success Message\n```\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### SupportServiceBridgeContract\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\External\\Beacon\\Contracts;\n\nuse Sovereign\\Bridge\\Contracts\\BoundaryContractInterface;\n\n/**\n * Specifically governs support and knowledge base boundary crossing.\n */\ninterface SupportServiceBridgeContract extends BoundaryContractInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Search and retrieve public-safe knowledge base content.\n     */\n    public function findArticles(string $query): All ticket updates are DTO-transformed at the Bridge.\n- **Content Filtering**: Only articles explicitly marked for \"Public\" visibility in ISPOKE-09 are accessible through this spoke.\n- **Attachment Security**: Uploaded files are scanned and sanitized at the Bridge before being passed to internal storage.\n- **UI Consistency**: Uses the \"Support\" component variants from `HUB-26`.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Article Isolation**: Automated tests must verify that \"Draft\" or \"Internal-only\" articles are never returned in search results.\n- **Ticket Ownership**: Verification that a customer cannot view or reply to a ticket ID belonging to another customer.\n- **Upload Integrity**: Malicious file types (e.g., .php, .exe) must be rejected at the Bridge with a `400 Bad Request`.\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Minor**. Extends the customer service capabilities of the platform.\n\n=== ESPOKE-12 ===\n# PHASE ESPOKE-12: Developer-Facing Public API Documentation Portal\n\n## Tier\nExternal Spoke (Public-facing Application)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Forge (Dev Portal)\n\n## Description\nThe primary destination for developers building on the Sovereign Stack. It dynamically generates and hosts documentation for both the Public REST API (ESPOKE-02) and the Public GraphQL API (ESPOKE-07). It provides interactive \"Try it now\" environments, SDK download links, and API key management.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nMust follow ESPOKE-02 (REST) and ESPOKE-07 (GraphQL) as it consumes their Storage)`\n\n## Architectural Design\n- **SchemaIntrospector**: Fetches the unified GraphQL schema from ESPOKE-07 and the OpenAPI spec from ESPOKE-02.\n- **DocGenerator**: A PHP-based engine that transforms schemas and Markdown files into a searchable documentation site.\n- **SandboxManager**: Provides an interactive UI for executing authenticated requests against the public APIs.\n- **DeveloperConsole**: A dashboard for developers to manage their public API keys and webhooks.\n\n### Documentation Generation Flow\n```mermaid\ngraph TD\n    S[ESPOKE-07: GraphQL Schema] --> I[Schema Introspector]\n    R[ESPOKE-02: REST OpenAPI] --> I\n    I --> G[Doc Generator]\n    M[Markdown Content] --> G\n    G --> P[Forge UI]\n    P --> U[Developer]\n    P --> B[BRIDGE-01: Bridge]\n    B --> K[DeveloperPortalBridgeContract]\n```\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### DeveloperPortalBridgeContract\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\External\\Forge\\Contracts;\n\nuse Sovereign\\Bridge\\Contracts\\BoundaryContractInterface;\n\n/**\n * Specifically governs developer account and API key management across the boundary.\n */\ninterface DeveloperPortalBridgeContract extends BoundaryContractInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Create or rotate a public API key for a developer.\n     */\n    public function manageApiKey(string $developerId, string $action): array;\n\n    /**\n     * Fetch developer-specific usage quotas and limits.\n     */\n    public function getApiUsage(string $developerId): array;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Dynamic Discovery**: Forge uses internal Hub-level service discovery (`HUB-15`) to find the active GraphQL and REST endpoints to pull fresh schemas.\n- **Bridge Compliance**: Developer API keys and quotas are managed via the `DeveloperPortalBridgeContract`.\n- **UI Consistency**: Uses the \"Developer\" theme of `HUB-26` (high-contrast code blocks, sidebar navigation).\n- **Security**: The \"Sandbox\" environment uses the user's actual API keys (stored in the session via `HUB-04`) to make real calls to the Gateway.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Doc Synchronization**: Documentation must automatically update within 60 seconds of a schema change in ESPOKE-02 or ESPOKE-07.\n- **Sandbox Isolation**: API requests made via the sandbox must be clearly tagged in `HUB-06` audit logs as \"Portal Sandbox\" requests.\n- **Performance**: Doc pages with large schemas (e.g., > 100 types) must render in < 500ms.\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Minor**. Improves developer experience and platform adoption.\n\n=== ESPOKE-13 ===\n# PHASE ESPOKE-13: Partner and Third-Party Integration Gateway\n\n## Tier\nExternal Spoke (Public-facing Application)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Bridgehead (Partner Gateway)\n\n## Description\nA specialized gateway for high-priority partner integrations and third-party webhooks. It provides dedicated endpoints, custom authentication logic for legacy partner systems, and an outbound webhook dispatcher to notify third-party systems of events within the Sovereign Stack.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nDepends on ESPOKE-12 (Developer Portal) for API key management and ESPOKE-02 (REST API) for base routing patterns.\n\n## Context7 Research\n### Direct Hub Dependencies\n- `HUB-08: API Gateway & Public Surface (Traffic Management)`\n- `HUB-06: Audit Log & Activity Tracker (Partner Auditing)`\n- `HUB-14: Distributed Task Queue (Webhook Dispatching)`\n- `HUB-04: Global Identity & Authentication (Partner Auth)`\n\n### Transitive Core Dependencies\n- `CORE-09: Cryptography & Hashing (Webhook Signing)`\n- `CORE-18: Core Kernel & Lifecycle (Event Loop)`\n- `CORE-04: HTTP Message (Payload Handling)`\n- `CORE-07: Event Dispatcher (Internal Event Capture)`\n\n## Architectural Design\n- **PartnerAuthManager**: Implements specialized auth strategies (e.g., mTLS, custom header signatures) for specific partner contracts.\n- **WebhookDispatcher**: Consumes internal events and pushes them to registered external partner URLs via `HUB-14`.\n- **PayloadTransformer**: Normalizes incoming partner data into Sovereign-safe DTOs before passing them to the Bridge.\n- **CircuitBreaker**: Protects the Sovereign Stack from slow or failing partner endpoints during webhook delivery.\n\n### Partner Integration Diagram\n```mermaid\ngraph LR\n    P[Partner System] --> B[ESPOKE-13: Bridgehead]\n    B --> G[BRIDGE-01: Bridge]\n    G --> I[Internal Spoke]\n    I --> E[CORE-07: Event]\n    E --> D[Webhook Dispatcher]\n    D --> W[HUB-14: Queue]\n    W --> P\n```\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### PartnerIntegrationBridgeContract\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\External\\Bridgehead\\Contracts;\n\nuse Sovereign\\Bridge\\Contracts\\BoundaryContractInterface;\n\n/**\n * Specifically governs high-privilege partner interactions across the boundary.\n */\ninterface PartnerIntegrationBridgeContract extends BoundaryContractInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Submit a partner-originated batch of data.\n     */\n    public function ingestPartnerData(string $partnerId, array $payload): array;\n\n    /**\n     * Register a partner's interest in specific internal event types.\n     */\n    public function registerWebhook(string $partnerId, string $url, array $events): void;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Bridge Compliance**: All partner data ingestion must pass through the `PartnerIntegrationBridgeContract` for transformation and policy enforcement.\n- **Webhook Security**: All outbound webhooks are signed with a HMAC-SHA256 signature using a partner-specific secret managed in `CORE-09`.\n- **Isolation**: Partner traffic is isolated from standard public API traffic via dedicated `HUB-08` route groups.\n- **Retry Logic**: Implements exponential backoff for failed webhook deliveries using `HUB-14`.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Signature Verification**: Every outbound webhook must be verified in tests to contain a valid `X-Sovereign-Signature` header.\n- **Circuit Breaking**: Verification that the system stops attempting to call a partner URL after 5 consecutive failures.\n- **Payload Sanitization**: Automated tests must confirm that partner payloads containing extra, non-contract fields are stripped at the Bridge.\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Minor**. Enables deep integration with the external business ecosystem.\n\n=== ESPOKE-14 ===\n# PHASE ESPOKE-14: Public Analytics and Reporting Endpoint\n\n## Tier\nExternal Spoke (Public-facing Application)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Lens (Analytics)\n\n## Description\nA dual-purpose analytics engine. It provides an ingestion endpoint for public client events (clicks, views, conversions) and a reporting interface for authenticated customers to view their own performance metrics. It bridges the gap between raw public activity and processed internal reporting data.\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nDepends on almost all other External Spokes as it collects data from them. It uses the reporting patterns established in ISPOKE-14 (Internal Analytics).\n\n## Context7 Research\n### Direct Hub Dependencies\n- `HUB-08: API Gateway & Public Surface (Ingestion Routing)`\n- `HUB-02: Distributed Cache (Real-time Counters)`\n- `HUB-26: Shared UI Component Library (Charts/Data Viz)`\n- `HUB-06: Audit Log & Activity Tracker (Raw Event Storage)`\n\n### Transitive Core Dependencies\n- `CORE-09: Cryptography & Hashing (Anonymization)`\n- `CORE-18: Core Kernel & Lifecycle (Processing Pipeline)`\n- `CORE-14: Filesystem Abstraction (Log Rotation)`\n- `CORE-11: SuperPHP Parser (Dynamic Reporting UI)`\n\n## Architectural Design\n- **EventIngestor**: A high-throughput, low-latency endpoint for receiving JSON analytics payloads.\n- **AnonymizationLayer**: Strips PII and salts identifiers using `CORE-09` before internal storage.\n- **MetricAggregator**: Consumes raw events and updates real-time counters in `HUB-02`.\n- **ReportingPresenter**: A customer-facing dashboard for viewing aggregated metrics using `HUB-26` visualization components.\n\n### Analytics Ingestion & Reporting Flow\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant U as Public Client\n    participant L as ESPOKE-14 (Lens)\n    participant B as BRIDGE-01 (Bridge)\n    participant I as Internal Spoke (ISPOKE-14: Insight)\n\n    Note over U, L: Ingestion Phase\n    U->>L: POST /collect (Event)\n    L->>L: Anonymize & Validate\n    L->>B: Dispatch Event (AnalyticsReportingBridgeContract)\n    B->>I: Record Raw Event\n\n    Note over U, L: Reporting Phase\n    U->>L: GET /reports (Dashboard)\n    L->>B: Get Aggregated Stats (AnalyticsReportingBridgeContract)\n    B->>I: Query Aggregates\n    I-->>B: Processed Data\n    B-->>L: Public-Safe Reporting DTO\n    L-->>U: Render Charts/Tables\n```\n\n## Interface Contracts\n\n### AnalyticsReportingBridgeContract\n```php\nnamespace Sovereign\\External\\Lens\\Contracts;\n\nuse Sovereign\\Bridge\\Contracts\\BoundaryContractInterface;\n\n/**\n * Specifically governs the analytics ingestion and reporting boundary.\n */\ninterface AnalyticsReportingBridgeContract extends BoundaryContractInterface\n{\n    /**\n     * Dispatch an anonymized event for internal storage and processing.\n     */\n    public function dispatchEvent(array $anonymizedData): void;\n\n    /**\n     * Fetch aggregated reporting data for a specific customer.\n     */\n    public function getCustomerReport(string $customerId, string $reportType, array $params): array;\n}\n```\n\n## Integration Strategy\n- **Bridge Compliance**: Raw event ingestion and reporting queries are strictly mediated by the `AnalyticsReportingBridgeContract`.\n- **Privacy First**: No raw IP addresses or user-agent strings cross the Bridge; they are anonymized within ESPOKE-14.\n- **Visualization**: Uses `HUB-26` primitives for SVG charts and data tables, ensuring consistent UI.\n- **Buffering**: High-volume ingestion events are buffered in `HUB-02` before being flushed to the Bridge in batches to protect internal service performance.\n\n## CI Verification Criteria\n- **Anonymization Test**: Verify that no PII (email, name, raw IP) is present in the `anonymizedData` payload sent to the Bridge.\n- **Ingestion Latency**: The `/collect` endpoint must return `202 Accepted` in < 10ms.\n- **Data Isolation**: Ensure Customer A cannot access aggregated reports belonging to Customer B.\n\n## SemVer Impact\n**Minor**. Provides transparency and data-driven insights to the public ecosystem.\n\n=== ESPOKE-15 ===\n# PHASE ESPOKE-15: External Spoke Orchestration and Health Reporting Layer\n\n## Tier\nExternal Spoke (Management & Orchestration)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Sentinel (External Orchestrator)\n\n## Description\nThe final phase of the External Spoke sub-tier. Sovereign Sentinel is an internal orchestration and health reporting layer that governs the entire public-facing sub-tier. it monitors the lifecycle, deployment state, and operational health of all External Spokes (ESPOKE-01 through ESPOKE-14), reporting back to the Core Orchestrator (CORE-01) and the Hub-level orchestration hooks (HUB-16).\n\n## Sequencing Rationale\nThe final phase (81 of 81). It must be the last component as its purpose is to oversee and coordinate the completed External sub-tier.\n\n## Context7 Research\n### Direct Hub Dependencies\n- `HUB-16: Hub-level Orchestration
