Change log\n","stderr":""}{"returncode":0,"stdout":"=== STRUCTURE-01-Wheel.md ===\n# STRUCTURE-01: Application Structure — The Sovereign Wheel\n\n**Status:** Canonical (v0.4 — consolidation merge).\n**Supersedes:** `Structure_01_Wheel_Architecture.md` (v0.1), `Structure_01_v0.2_Wheel_Architecture.md`\n(v0.2), `STRUCTURE-01.md` (interim), `STRUCTURE-01-v0.3.md` (v0.3) — all four now archived under\n`Analysis_Critiques_Rewrites/`.\n**Numbering authority:** `INDEX.md` §2. Where this document names a component, `INDEX.md` wins.\n\n> **Consolidation note (`Verification/INCONSISTENCIES.md` #9).** Four versions of this document existed\n> with three mutually incompatible depth scales and two competing ring vocabularies (*Inner/Outer Rim*\n> vs *Thick/Thin Spokes*). v0.3 was the most correct model but dropped the Pulse 6-tuple formalism and\n> the directory/ID map that `STRUCTURE-02`–`STRUCTURE-09` depend on. This file keeps **v0.3's model as\n> normative** (Part A), re-attaches **v0.2's Pulse formalism** (Part B) and **v0.1's ID map** (Part C),\n> and adds a single **normative vocabulary** (Part D) that `CrossCutting/GLOSSARY.md` mirrors verbatim.\n\n---\n\n# Part A — The model (normative, from v0.3)\n\n## A.1 The core distinction: layer vs. checkpoint\n\nEverything else in this model falls out of one test, applied consistently:\n\n> **A ring is a layer if it has a catalog of independently-stateful members. A ring is a checkpoint if\n> it is a single, stateless enforcement contract.**\n\nApplied to the six rings:\n\n| Ring | Members | Layer or checkpoint? |\n|---|---|---|\n| Core | `CORE-01`–`20`, each independently versioned | Layer |\n| Hub | `HUB-01`–`30` (+ `HUB-31` if ADR-011 is accepted), each independently stateful | Layer |\n| Inner Spoke | `ISPOKE-01`–`25` | Layer |\n| **Inner Rim** | **`BRIDGE-01`** — one component, no schema of its own, entire spec is transform-and-forward rules | **Checkpoint** |\n| Outer Spoke | `ESPOKE-01`–`15` | Layer |\n| **Outer Rim** | **`HUB-08`** Gateway — stateless enforcement (auth, throttle, CORS, routing) | **Checkpoint** |\n\nFour layers, two checkpoints — not six equivalent rings. This is the load-bearing distinction for\neverything below.\n\n> `HUB-31` is **proposed, not accepted** — see `ADRs/ADR-011-hub-31-real-time-analytics.md` and\n> `OPEN-DECISIONS.md`. The Hub ring is `HUB-01`–`30` until that ADR is accepted.\n\n## A.2 Connectivity rules\n\n- **Free lateral mesh within any layer.** Confirmed against real dependency graphs, not asserted:\n  `HUB-05`→`HUB-04`, `HUB-08`→`HUB-04`+`HUB-07`, `HUB-17` alone touches four other Hub services,\n  `ISPOKE-08`'s own sequencing depends on `ISPOKE-07`, `CORE-18`→`CORE-02`+`CORE-08`+`CORE-09`+`CORE-10`.\n  Lateral traffic inside a layer is the *majority* of real traffic, not an edge case.\n- **Free radial flow between adjacent layers — except across a checkpoint.** Core↔Hub and\n  Hub↔Inner-Spoke are unrestricted. Outer-Spoke↔Inner-Spoke is **not** free — it crosses the Inner Rim,\n  which is the entire reason `BRIDGE-01` exists. Stating the rule without this exception makes it false\n  at the one place it matters most.\n\n## A.3 Hub's status: mandatory layer, not a checkpoint\n\nTwo claims that look contradictory resolve once \"mandatory to traverse\" and \"enforced\" are separated:\n\n- **Mandatory to traverse** — architectural: nothing skips the Hub to reach Core directly. This is a\n  routing/dependency-graph property.\n- **Enforced checkpoint** — behavioral: stops a request and can reject it.\n\nHub is the first, not the second. Concretely: every Hub-tier write passes through `HUB-06` (Auditor),\nwhich **records unconditionally** — it does not block. Authorization that *can* block\n(`HUB-04`/`HUB-05`) applies to Hub calls **originating from public-entity context**; it does not\nre-gate calls already inside the trust boundary (Hub-to-Hub, Internal-Spoke-to-Hub). So: Hub is a\nmandatory layer with an always-on recorder and a conditional gate that only activates for\npublic-originated traffic passing through it — not a second Bridge.\n\n*(Framing this as distinct \"Audit\" and \"Authz\" sub-mechanisms is a description of what `HUB-06` and\n`HUB-04`/`HUB-05` already do in the blueprints; it is adopted descriptively and does not assume a\ndeeper \"Facet\" architecture beyond what is stated here.)*\n\n## A.4 Thickness: consequence, not definition\n\nThe canonical classifier for Internal vs. External Spoke is **who calls it** — staff-authenticated vs.\npublic. Thickness (breadth of unmediated Hub/Core access) is a *consequence* of that, not the test\nitself: a staff tool with narrow access is still Internal because of its caller, even if it looks\n\"thin\" by access breadth. Do not classify by thickness and back into trust level; classify by trust\nlevel and expect thickness to follow. Confirmed against real files: `ISPOKE-01` has seven direct,\nunmediated Hub dependencies plus direct Core access; every `ESPOKE-*` is capped to a narrow,\npublic-safe Hub subset and is structurally required to route through `BRIDGE-01` for anything internal.\n\n## A.5 Entity classes and entry points\n\nThe model needs two entry points, not one:\n\n- **Public entity** → enters at the **Outer Rim**, gated by `HUB-08` (auth, throttle, CORS). Full\n  radial traversal from there, subject to the checkpoint rule in §A.2.\n- **Staff entity** → enters at a **separate gate at the Inner Spoke layer**, not by traversing the\n  Outer Rim and \"skipping a few rings.\" This has its own authentication path: `ISPOKE-04` (Sovereign\n  Staff Hub — SSO, MFA enrollment, internal identity), distinct from the public JWT/session flow\n  `HUB-04` runs for Bridge-mediated traffic. Two gates, two trust domains, one system.\n\n## A.6 Depth is two variables, not one\n\n\"How deep it goes depends\" collapses two independent decisions into one word:\n\n- **Entry radius** — set by entity class (§A.5). Not negotiable per-request; it is which gate you came\n  through.\n- **Traversal depth from entry** — set by **data/capability locality**, not by the requester's auth\n  level. A public customer requesting their own order history still only ever reaches internal data\n  through `BRIDGE-01`, regardless of how \"deep\" that data lives. Traversal depth for public traffic is\n  **an architecture decision about where the data lives**, not a permissions escalation — the\n  difference between a data-placement decision (cheap to change) and an authorization model (expensive\n  to get wrong).\n\n## A.7 Pulse taxonomy — reflection symmetry is the exception\n\nExemplar One (§A.9) is the **synchronous radial** case, not the general model. It breaks in the one\nplace that matters most in the actual system: `HUB-17` (Webhook Nexus) receiving a Stripe payment\nwebhook — shallow, synchronous acknowledgment (200 OK) at entry, then the real effect propagates\nasynchronously through `HUB-09`→`HUB-22`→`ISPOKE-13`→`HUB-06`, with the pulse the customer actually\n*perceives* exiting later, through a different spoke entirely (`ESPOKE-06`/`ISPOKE-07` notification),\non a different timeline, with no mirrored return path through `HUB-17` at all.\n\nThree named types:\n\n| Type | Shape | Example |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A — Synchronous radial** | In and out, same path, same request/response cycle | Exemplar One (§A.9) |\n| **B — Asymmetric fan-out** | Shallow entry, deep + delayed + multi-modal exit via a different spoke | `HUB-17` Stripe webhook → billing chain → notification exit |\n| **C — Origin-less / system-initiated** | No inbound leg — originates at Hub/Core, radiates outward | `HUB-25` Chronos-triggered job with no requesting entity |\n\nA structure model that only accounts for Type A will misrepresent most production traffic.\n\n## A.8 HUB-31 — commit or decide, do not silently strike\n\n`HUB-31` (Real-Time Analytics & Metrics Ledger) is not a placeholder invented for this diagram. It is a\nregistered, justified gap with three specific, independently-described dependents (`ISPOKE-05`,\n`ISPOKE-12`, `ISPOKE-13`) that each need genuine real-time streaming metrics no other Hub component\nprovides. `HUB-15` (Health) is scoped to operational/service health, not business metrics — it is not a\nsubstitute, and redirecting `HUB-31`'s dependents to it would reintroduce the exact mislabeling this\naudit exists to fix. `HUB-23` (Reporter) is async batch export — also not a substitute.\n\n**Phase-1 status:** `HUB-31` is **proposed**, tracked in `ADRs/ADR-011-hub-31-real-time-analytics.md`\nand `OPEN-DECISIONS.md`. It is *not* counted in the Hub tier total in `INDEX.md` §4 until accepted.\nDependents cite it as `pending`.\n\n## A.9 Exemplar One (confirmed)\n\n**Type A — synchronous radial.** A public visitor requests a knowledge-base article that is not cached.\n\n```\nEntity (browser)\n  → Outer Rim (HUB-08 Gateway: auth/throttle/CORS)\n  → Outer Spoke (ESPOKE-01 Sovereign Canvas: ContentConsumer)\n  → Inner Rim (BRIDGE-01 Vanguard: transform + re-validate)\n  → Inner Spoke (ISPOKE-09 Sovereign Codex: isPublic() check, fetch)\n  ← Inner Rim (BRIDGE-01: DTO transform back to public-safe shape)\n  ← Outer Spoke (ESPOKE-01: render)\n  ← Outer Rim (HUB-08: response)\n← Entity\n```\n\nDepth reached: Inner Spoke layer. Does not touch Core directly in its own control flow — *depth* means\nthe request's own routing path, not the full transitive dependency graph of whichever component serves\nit.\n\n## A.10 Open\n\nExemplar Two (Type B) — the `HUB-17`→`HUB-09`→`HUB-22`→`ISPOKE-13`→`HUB-06`→`ESPOKE-06`/`ISPOKE-07`\nchain with the asymmetric exit drawn explicitly — is not yet traced. Tracked in `OPEN-DECISIONS.md`.\n\n---\n\n# Part B — Pulse formalism (normative, re-attached from v0.2)\n\n`STRUCTURE-02`–`STRUCTURE-09` reference the Pulse tuple, the Pulse classes, and the axioms below. v0.3\ndropped them; they are restored here unchanged in substance, with ring names normalised to Part D.\n\n## B.1 Pulse definition (6-tuple)\n\n```\nPulse = (\n  entity,         -- who/what touched the entry gate (user ULID | system | anonymous)\n  entry_spoke,    -- spoke of entry            (http | cli | ws | amqp | internal)\n  depth,          -- deepest ring reached      (see Part D: Depth scale)\n  exit_spoke,     -- spoke of exit             (may differ from entry_spoke)\n  lane,           -- tenant scope              (tenant ULID | system | public)\n  pulse_class     -- live | dormant | purge | ignition\n)\n```\n\nAll six fields are non-nullable. The `exit_spoke` field is what makes **asymmetric exit** (Pulse\nType B, §A.7) expressible: a Pulse entering via HTTP may exit via the event bus (`HUB-09`).\n\n## B.2 Pulse classes\n\n| Class | Description | Identity rules | Authorization rules |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `live` | Standard synchronous request/response | Entity-minted JWT (ES256, `HUB-04`) | Full Hub authorization enforced |\n| `dormant` | Suspended saga / workflow continuation | Original entity preserved | Re-evaluated on resume |\n| `purge` | System-issued cache invalidation or config propagation | `system` | Bypasses authorization; still audited |\n| `ignition` | Boot / shutdown sequence | `system:boot` | Bypasses authorization; audit buffers via `CORE-03` |\n\n## B.3 Axioms\n\n1. **No skipping rings.** A Pulse cannot jump from the Outer Rim to the Inner Spoke layer. Each ring\n   crossing is recorded by `HUB-06`.\n2. **Inward-only calls at runtime.** A Core component never calls outward at runtime. Static\n   dependencies may point inward from any ring.\n3. **Hub is mandatory for Core crossings.** No Spoke touches Core directly. Every Core access from a\n   Spoke is wrapped in a Hub transaction envelope with audit.\n4. **Depth is immutable after commitment.** The Inner Rim sets depth; deeper rings cannot escalate it\n   silently.\n5. **Every Pulse is auditable.** All six tuple fields are recorded at the Hub on every Core crossing.\n6. **Tangential flow stays at or above the Inner Rim.** Pulses travelling between Inner Spokes use\n   `HUB-08` along the Inner Rim; they never drop to Outer Spokes or the Outer Rim.\n7. **Reverse Pulses are system-issued.** Outward-travelling Pulses (cache invalidation, config\n   propagation) have `entity = system` and `pulse_class = purge`.\n8. **Ignition Pulses bypass authorization only.** Boot sequences use `pulse_class = ignition`; the\n   audit path buffers events via `CORE-03` rather than skipping them.\n\n---\n\n# Part C — Blueprint-to-wheel map (from v0.1, re-anchored to `INDEX.md` §2)\n\n## C.1 Ring assignment\n\n| Blueprint IDs | Wheel position | Spoke thickness | Access |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `CORE-01`–`CORE-20` | **Core** (nucleus) | n/a | System only |\n| `HUB-01`–`HUB-30` (+`HUB-31` pending) | **Hub** (inner ring) | n/a | Internal services |\n| `BRIDGE-01` | **Inner Rim** (the gate) | n/a | Traffic controller |\n| `ISPOKE-01`–`ISPOKE-25` | **Inner Spokes** | Thick | Staff-only (authenticated + authorized) |\n| `ESPOKE-01`–`ESPOKE-15` | **Outer Spokes** | Thin | Public (anonymous or authenticated) |\n| `HUB-08` | **Outer Rim** (the edge checkpoint) | n/a | Public ingress enforcement |\n| `DEPLOY-00`–`DEPLOY-04` | **Frame** (infrastructure) | n/a | Infrastructure |\n\n> `HUB-08` appears twice on purpose: it is a Hub-tier *package* and simultaneously the Outer Rim\n> *checkpoint*. That is not a numbering conflict; it is the one component whose ring role differs from\n> its tier.\n\n## C.2 Deploy as the frame\n\n| Deploy component | Frame function |\n|---|---|\n| `DEPLOY-00` Documentation Site | The manual bolted to the frame |\n| `DEPLOY-01` Core & Hub Service Deployment | The hub mount — keeps the Hub ring centred |\n| `DEPLOY-02` Datastore Provisioning | The axle bearings — PostgreSQL 16 + Redis 7 |\n| `DEPLOY-03` Bridge & External Spoke Deployment | The rim clamps — secures the Outer Rim |\n| `DEPLOY-04` Multi-Environment & Promotion Pipeline | The maintenance lift |\n\n## C.3 Repository layout\n\n> **ADR-001 (polyrepo).** The tree below is a *logical* map, not a single repository. Each leaf under\n> `packages/`, `spokes/`, and `bridge/` is an **independent repository** (~50+ in total), aggregated\n> for local development by the polyrepo workspace root `composer.json` that `CORE-20` (Forge) writes\n> and `CORE-01` (Loom) orchestrates. `STRUCTURE-06`, `STRUCTURE-07`, and `STRUCTURE-08` show\n> per-repository layouts on the same understanding.\n\n```\n<polyrepo workspace>\n├── packages/core/            CORE-01..20            (one repo per package)\n├── packages/hub/             HUB-01..30             (one repo per service)\n├── bridge/vanguard/          BRIDGE-01              ★ INNER RIM — The Gate\n├── spokes/internal/          ISPOKE-01..25          ★ INNER SPOKES — Thick (staff)\n├── spokes/external/          ESPOKE-01..15          ★ OUTER SPOKES — Thin (public)\n└── deploy/                   DEPLOY-00..04          ★ FRAME\n    ├── terraform/            DEPLOY-02  (PostgreSQL 16, Redis 7 — ADR-006/ADR-007)\n    ├── kubernetes/           DEPLOY-01, DEPLOY-03\n    └── pipelines/            DEPLOY-04\n```\n\n> **Directory-name caution.** v0.1 of this document also carried a per-spoke folder-name column\n> (`admin/`, `devportal/`, `codex/`, …). Those folder names disagree with the `## Component Name`\n> fields in the actual `ISPOKE-*` / `ESPOKE-*` blueprints for several IDs, and the v0.1 names for\n> `ISPOKE-16`–`25` disagree with `docs/internal-spokes/placeholder-blueprints.md`. The column is\n> therefore **not** reproduced here. `INDEX.md` §2 is the only naming authority; the open naming\n> question is recorded in `OPEN-DECISIONS.md`.\n\n---\n\n# Part D — Normative vocabulary\n\n`CrossCutting/GLOSSARY.md` mirrors this section. Any other definition in the tree is stale.\n\n| Term | Definition |\n|---|---|\n| **Wheel** | The whole system, modelled as six concentric rings around a Core nucleus. |\n| **Ring** | One concentric band of the Wheel. Six exist: Core, Hub, Inner Rim, Inner Spoke, Outer Rim, Outer Spoke. |\n| **Layer** | A ring with a catalog of independently-stateful members (Core, Hub, Inner Spoke, Outer Spoke). |\n| **Checkpoint** | A ring that is a single, stateless enforcement contract (Inner Rim = `BRIDGE-01`; Outer Rim = `HUB-08`). |\n| **Rim** | A checkpoint ring. **Inner Rim** = `BRIDGE-01`; **Outer Rim** = `HUB-08`. The words *Inner Rim* / *Outer Rim* are the only sanctioned rim names. |\n| **Spoke** | A member of the Inner Spoke or Outer Spoke layer. |\n| **Thick / Thin** | A *property* of a spoke (breadth of unmediated Hub/Core access), never a ring name. Inner Spokes are thick, Outer Spokes are thin — as a consequence of caller trust (§A.4), not as the classifier. |\n| **Entity** | The originator of a Pulse: a public entity, a staff entity, or `system`. |\n| **Entry radius** | Which gate an entity entered through — Outer Rim (public) or the Inner-Spoke staff gate `ISPOKE-04` (staff). Fixed by entity class. |\n| **Depth** | The deepest ring a Pulse's own routing path reaches. Measured on the single scale in §D.1 — the three competing scales in v0.1/v0.2/v0.3 are withdrawn. |\n| **Traversal depth** | Depth measured *from the entry radius*, determined by data/capability locality, not by the requester's authorization level. |\n| **Pulse** | One unit of runtime work, described by the 6-tuple in §B.1. **Reserved word:** no component may be named \"Sovereign Pulse\" except `HUB-15`. |\n| **Lane** | The tenant scope of a Pulse: a tenant ULID, `system`, or `public`. |\n| **Pulse class** | One of `live`, `dormant`, `purge`, `ignition` (§B.2). |\n| **Pulse type** | One of A (synchronous radial), B (asymmetric fan-out), C (origin-less) (§A.7). |\n\n## D.1 The single depth scale\n\nv0.1, v0.2, and v0.3 each used a different numbering. This is the only sanctioned scale:\n\n| Depth | Ring reached |\n|---|---|\n| 1 | Outer Rim (`HUB-08`) |\n| 2 | Outer Spoke (`ESPOKE-*`) |\n| 3 | Inner Rim (`BRIDGE-01`) |\n| 4 | Inner Spoke (`ISPOKE-*`) |\n| 5 | Hub (`HUB-*`) |\n| 6 | Core (`CORE-*`) |\n\nA staff-entity Pulse enters at depth 4 by definition (§A.5) and never has depths 1–3.\n\n## D.2 Reserved component names\n\n| Reserved name | Sole owner | Note |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Sovereign Pulse** | `HUB-15` (Health Check & Service Discovery) | `HUB-09` was renamed to **Sovereign Signal (Event Bus)** to clear this collision (`Verification/INCONSISTENCIES.md` #9). |\n| **Pulse** (bare) | the unit-of-work concept (§B.1) | Never a component name. |\n\nRemaining soft collisions — \"Sovereign Forge\" (`CORE-20`, `ISPOKE-02`, `ISPOKE-11`, `ESPOKE-12`),\n\"Sovereign Nexus\" (`HUB-17`, `HUB-21`, `ISPOKE-14`, `ESPOKE-07`), \"Sovereign Sentinel\" (`HUB-27`,\n`ESPOKE-15`), \"Sovereign Ledger\" (`HUB-22`, `ISPOKE-13`) — are disambiguated by their parenthetical\nqualifiers in `INDEX.md` §2 and are recorded as an open naming question in `OPEN-DECISIONS.md`. ## What landed

Two commits today. The second (`19bb1cb2`) is the real one: a full consolidation of **multiple parallel critique efforts** — mine (Finding numbers cited directly, my v0.3 structure doc quoted verbatim and adopted as normative), plus at least one other pass evidenced by the file names in the first commit (`Kimi.md`, `Z.ai... GLM-5.2` exports) — merged into a new canonical `Architecture/` tree: `Core/`, `Hub/`, `Spoke/{Internal,External,Bridge}/`, `Deploy/`, 10 accepted `ADRs/` + 1 proposed, 9 `CrossCutting/` structure docs, an `INDEX.md` governance authority, and — this is the part worth taking seriously — a **working CI linter** (`Verification/lint/run.php`) that re-derives every cross-reference against `INDEX.md` and fails the build on a mismatch. That's the exact mechanism I could only recommend as a "should exist" in Governance Rule 1; someone actually built it.

## Where my work landed, specifically

- `STRUCTURE-01-Wheel.md`'s Part A is my v0.3 verbatim, explicitly kept as "the most correct model."
- `DEPLOY-00` rename, `HUB-31` as commit-not-strike (now `ADR-011`, correctly still marked *Proposed* not *Accepted* — six dependents cite it as `pending`), the archival-banner approach — all match what I proposed, independently arrived at or adopted directly.
- All 8 of my cross-reference mislabel patterns are in `INDEX.md` §3, credited as Findings 3/15.

## What it caught in *my* work that I missed

I gave both `HUB-09` and `HUB-15` the component name "Sovereign Pulse" in my own rewrites — I was auditing dependency references, not doing a global name-collision sweep, so I never caught it. They did: `HUB-09` is renamed "Sovereign Signal," and "Sovereign Pulse" is now reserved for `HUB-15` only, since "Pulse" also became the reserved architectural noun for the wheel/pulse model. Worth owning plainly rather than glossing over — good catch on their part. They also flag "Sovereign Forge" as a four-way collision (`CORE-20`, `ISPOKE-02`, `ISPOKE-11`, `ESPOKE-12`) that neither of us resolved — still open in `OPEN-DECISIONS.md` (OD-05). I did catch the Ledger/Nexus collisions myself, so it's not a clean miss across the board, but the Pulse one is a real gap I should've caught.

## Two things beyond either of our critiques

Genuinely new findings, not duplicated from mine: a **datastore conflict** — MySQL was canonical everywhere prior; `ADR-007` now mandates PostgreSQL 16 + JSONB + ULID, and the whole `STRUCTURE-05/07/08/09` set was rewritten around it. That's a substantive infrastructure pivot, not a documentation fix — worth confirming that's actually the direction you want before it's load-bearing everywhere. And a **dependency-DAG cycle** (`HUB-03`↔`HUB-11` each listed as the other's downstream) that neither of us caught, now fixed with binding edge-direction semantics in `INDEX.md` §5.1.

## Where this leaves us

My local package (`/mnt/user-data/outputs/DGLab-Blueprints-v2/`) and the `STRUCTURE-01-v0.3.md` we were iterating on are both effectively superseded now — the repo has its own authoritative `INDEX.md` and `STRUCTURE-01-Wheel.md`, and other processes are actively writing to it. Continuing to develop my local copy in parallel would just recreate the exact fragmentation problem this whole exercise exists to fix.

Given that: do you want me to stop treating my local package as the working copy and instead read/write directly against `Architecture/` in the actual repo going forward? If so, the natural next task is sitting right there in their own doc — **Exemplar Two (Type B, the `HUB-17` webhook fan-out trace)** is explicitly marked open in both `STRUCTURE-01-Wheel.md` §A.10 and `OPEN-DECISIONS.md`. I can trace it directly against their now-canonical model rather than my archived v0.3.

**MySQL 8.0+ with the InnoDB storage engine is now the primary\nrelational datastore** for the Sovereign Stack. PostgreSQL is **relegated behind the CORE-19 driver\nabstraction**: the PostgreSQL driver remains implemented in the DBAL but is **disabled by default** and\nis only activated at a later \"decision scale\" (an explicit future decision), not by default today.\n\nThis change is driven by operational and hosting realities: MySQL is the default managed database\nacross the PHP hosting ecosystem, the team's operational tooling/experience centers on MySQL/InnoDB,\nand the marginal feature gains PostgreSQL provided (RLS, `JSONB`, partial indexes) are satisfiable\nthrough the driver/abstraction layer plus application-level controls (see Consequences). Keeping\nPostgreSQL *available through the driver* preserves the option to re-promote it later without a\nre-architecture.\n\n## Decision\n\n- **Primary datastore:** MySQL 8.0+ (InnoDB). All blueprints, migrations, and CI test fixtures assume\n  MySQL/InnoDB semantics as the reference.\n- **Driver model (CORE-19):** CORE-19 exposes a `DriverInterface`. The **MySQL/InnoDB driver is enabled\n  by default**. The **PostgreSQL driver is shipped but disabled** (`enabled: false` in the default\n  `core.database.drivers` config) and is activated only by an explicit future decision at the next\n  scale boundary. SQLite remains the test/dev fixture (single-writer only).\n- **Feature detection:** Every engine-specific capability is gated behind\n  `DriverInterface::supports(string $feature): bool`. Code must degrade gracefully or fail loud on a\n  driver that does not support a requested feature — never assume PostgreSQL semantics.\n- **Keys:** `ULID` (ADR-009) primary keys are stored as `CHAR(26) CHARACTER SET ascii` in MySQL; the\n  app/DBAL generates ULIDs (no native engine type needed).\n- **Document storage:** structured/JSON data uses the MySQL 8 `JSON` column type, queryable via\n  generated columns + functional indexes (HUB-01 feature-flag overrides).\n- **Multi-tenant isolation (HUB-21):** enforced by the DBAL's mandatory `tenant_id` context\n  (injected into every query via CORE-19 + HUB-21), not by engine-level RLS. A forgot-the-`WHERE`\n  risk is contained by the DBAL, which refuses to execute a tenant-scoped statement without the tenant\n  context.\n\n## Alternatives Considered\n\n| Alternative | Pros | Cons | Verdict |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **MySQL 8.0+ (InnoDB)** | Default managed DB in the PHP ecosystem; mature tooling (Percona, `pt-online-schema-change`); team operational expertise; `JSON` type + generated/functional indexes; `CHAR(26)` ULID PKs; online DDL | No native Row-Level Security (mitigated by DBAL tenant context); `JSON` is parsed on read (acceptable at our volume); no SQL-standard partial indexes (use generated-column + index) | **Accepted — primary** |\n| **PostgreSQL 16+ (relegated)** | RLS, `JSONB` + GIN, partial indexes, `STORED` generated cols | Operational/ hosting friction for this team; the feature gap is bridgeable via the driver + app-level controls | Retained as a **disabled DBAL driver**; re-promote only at the next decision scale |\n| **SQLite** | Zero-admin, ideal test fixture | Single-writer lock; not multi-node | Kept as test/dev fixture only |\n| **MariaDB** | MySQL-compatible, cleaner license | Same limitations as MySQL; fork divergence | Allowed where an existing install must be supported (uses the MySQL driver) |\n\n## Consequences\n\n**Positive:**\n- Aligns the datastore with the team's operational reality and the default managed-MySQL hosting\n  ecosystem — lower operational risk day one.\n- The driver boundary means PostgreSQL is one config flip away from being re-enabled; no code rewrite\n  is needed when the next decision scale re-evaluates it.\n- `ULID` (ADR-009) and tenant scoping (HUB-21) are engine-agnostic, so the primary-key and\n  multi-tenancy strategies are unchanged by this reversal.\n\n**Negative (and how they are contained):**\n- **No native RLS.** Tenant isolation is enforced by the DBAL tenant context (CORE-19 + HUB-21), which\n  rejects tenant-scoped queries missing the context. This is application-enforced, not DB-enforced; it\n  is the documented trade-off of this decision.\n- **`JSON` read-parsing + no GIN.** HUB-01 feature-flag queries use a generated column + functional\n  index over the JSON path; acceptable at current volume. `DriverInterface::supports('jsonb')` returns\n  `false` on MySQL and code must use the generated-column path.\n- **No SQL-standard partial indexes.** HUB-06 audit-log performance uses a generated `deleted_at IS\n  NULL` boolean column indexed normally; `DriverInterface::supports('partial_index')` is `false` on\n  MySQL, so migrations must not emit PostgreSQL `WHERE` partial-index DDL.\n- **Dialect translation.** The DBAL query builder emits MySQL-compatible SQL by default and translates\n  for the (disabled) PostgreSQL driver only when that driver is enabled.\n\n**Neutral:**\n- Every Hub blueprint using an engine-specific feature must call `DriverInterface::supports()` and\n  degrade or fail explicitly.\n- Migrations live per-repo (`database/migrations/`) and are versioned per SemVer; a breaking schema\n  change is a major bump for the owning repo.\n\n## Links\n- Supersedes: ADR-007 (PostgreSQL primary)\n- Related: ADR-006 (Redis — companion non-relational store), ADR-009 (ULID keys), ADR-001 (polyrepo —\n  migrations per-repo), CORE-19 (Sovereign DBAL — driver registry), HUB-21 (Sovereign Nexus — tenant\n  context), HUB-01 (Config — JSON via generated column), HUB-06 (Audit — generated-column index)\n","stderr":""}{"returncode":0,"stdout":"=== ISPOKE-16 (promoted from placeholder) ===\n# PHASE ISPOKE-16: Sovereign Transporter (Import/Export)\n\n## Tier\nInternal Spoke (Staff-only application — VPN/bastion, never via the public Bridge)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Transporter — `SovereignStack\\Internal\\Transporter`. ## The new commit: `4d336589`, this morning

**`ADR-013` reverses `ADR-007`: MySQL 8 (InnoDB) is now the primary datastore, PostgreSQL is relegated to a disabled-by-default driver.** This isn't a careless flip-flop — it's a genuinely well-reasoned ADR. `CORE-19` gets a `DriverInterface::supports(string $feature): bool` gate, so code can't silently assume PostgreSQL-only semantics (RLS, JSONB+GIN) on MySQL. The RLS trade-off specifically is handled well: tenant isolation moves to a DBAL-enforced mandatory `tenant_id` context (rejects tenant-scoped queries missing it) rather than relying on an engine feature MySQL doesn't have. PostgreSQL stays one config flip away if it's re-promoted later — they didn't burn the bridge, they just stopped building on it as primary. Same commit also promoted all 13 remaining placeholder blueprints (`ISPOKE-16`–`25`, `DEPLOY-02`–`04`) to full-fidelity, closing out Finding 13's under-count for good.

## One thing I caught spot-checking it

`ISPOKE-16.md`'s own description still reads *"written via the canonical persistence path (CORE-19 Database over MySQL 16 / JSONB)"* — that's a leftover from the PostgreSQL era, half-converted: "16" is PostgreSQL's version number, and "JSONB" is Postgres-only syntax; MySQL uses plain `JSON` (which `ADR-013` itself states correctly). Every other file I checked (`DEPLOY-02`, `ADR-013` itself) converted cleanly — this looks like one line that slipped through the mass find-and-replace across 42 files. Small, but it's exactly the class of bug this whole audit exists to catch, so worth flagging rather than letting it sit.

## State of things

`HUB-31` (`OPEN-DECISIONS.md` OD-01) is still genuinely open — `ADR-011` wasn't touched by this commit beyond a superseded-reference update, still `Proposed`, still `pending` in its six dependents. That one's a real architecture-lead decision, not something that resolves itself via cleanup.

Given how fast this repo is moving on its own — two substantive commits in under 24 hours, clearly other people/processes actively working it — do you want me to keep doing periodic check-ins like this, or is there something specific you want me to pick up directly? The `ISPOKE-16` fix is a one-line PR if you want it; `HUB-31`/OD-01 needs an actual decision, not a check.

```
In the DGLab repo, under Architecture/Spoke/Internal/, the files ISPOKE-16.md through
ISPOKE-25.md each contain a leftover reference to "MySQL 16 / JSONB" in their Description
section — a wrap-around artifact from before ADR-013 (Architecture/ADRs/ADR-013-mysql-primary-datastore.md)

number and "JSONB" is PostgreSQL-only syntax; per ADR-013, MySQL uses plain JSON.

Task: in each of ISPOKE-16.md through ISPOKE-25.md, replace "MySQL 16 / JSONB" (and any
equivalent wrapped/split version of that phrase) with "MySQL 8 (InnoDB) / JSON" — matching the
phrasing already used correctly in Architecture/Deploy/DEPLOY-02.md and ADR-013 itself.

  CORE-19.md — those are legitimately describing the disabled PostgreSQL driver as an
  alternative, not describing MySQL's current behavior. Only fix instances that describe
  MySQL's own current capability incorrectly.

Scope this to exactly that text fix across those 10 files — no other edits, no reformatting,
no touching unrelated content in the same files. Show me a diff before committing.
```

Why it's written that way: names the exact files so the agent doesn't have to search-and-guess, gives it the *why* (so it can sanity-check ambiguous cases like the `JSONB` mentions in the six files that are fine), and explicitly fences off what not to touch — agents left unscoped tend to "helpfully" reformat or fix unrelated things while they're in the file, which turns a one-line fix into a noisy diff you then have to review line by line. The "show me a diff before committing" at the end keeps a human check in the loop before it hits git history.

**[USER]**

Q: Where does this splash screen live?
A: Wherever necessary

---

**[ASSISTANT]**

Fair enough — that answer's actually informative: it means the right move is a policy that scales across surfaces, not a one-off decision. Here's how I'd think about both.

**Splash screens — mostly, don't.**

The right lens is frequency of use. A splash screen is a one-time toll on load time in exchange for a moment of brand presence — that trade only pays off when the surface is used rarely or the load genuinely needs bridging.

- **Public site (`ESPOKE-01` Canvas):** No. Both `ESPOKE-01.md` and `ESPOKE-05.md` already have a Lighthouse-100/SEO CI gate baked into their benchmark methodology — a splash directly fights that criterion by delaying LCP for zero functional reason. Skip it.
- **Admin Panel (`ISPOKE-01`):** No, for the same reason but a different metric — staff open this many times a day, so a splash becomes accumulated friction, not brand presence. Use a skeleton loader for the genuinely async parts (SSO redirect, MFA check via `HUB-04`) instead of a full splash — it fills the same "don't show a blank screen" need without the ceremony.
- **CLI (`s-cli`):** The one place a splash is arguably earned, but scope it narrowly — a one-line banner (name, version, docs link) on `s-cli` with no args or `s-cli about`, never on every invocation. A big ASCII banner on every command is exactly the kind of thing that breaks the moment someone scripts against the CLI in CI (which `CORE-01`/Loom already does — see `orchestrator/ci/run.php`). If you want brand presence in the terminal, put it in `--version`/`about`, not the hot path.

**Microcopy — this is the part worth actually formalizing, and it maps onto infrastructure that already exists.**

Handwriting copy per-Spoke is the same failure mode this entire session has been finding and fixing in the docs — duplicated, drifting, no single source of truth. Two pieces already in the corrected blueprint set are built for exactly this:

1. **`HUB-13` (I18n & L10n)** should own every user-facing string as a translation key from day one — including strings that will only ever have one locale for now. Route microcopy through `trans()` even in English-only surfaces; it means you never do a second pass to "make it translatable" later, and it gives you the single source of truth by construction rather than by discipline.
2. **`HUB-26`'s `ThemeInterface`** (`tokens()` / `componentOverrides()`) governs visual tokens per theme (Admin vs. Public). The natural extension is a sibling — a copy/tone contract per theme, not per Spoke: Internal Spoke tone (staff, technical, terse — "Delete tenant?" not "Are you sure you'd like to remove this tenant?") vs. External Spoke tone (public, warmer, plainer language, more reassurance on destructive actions). Define that split once at the `HUB-26` theme level, and every `ISPOKE`/`ESPOKE` inherits it instead of each one inventing its own voice.

Concretely, that gives you a short, real style guide — tone per theme, a handful of reusable patterns (empty states, error messages, confirmations, success toasts), and the rule that no Spoke hardcodes copy, everything resolves through `HUB-13`. That's a half-page doc, not a blueprint-sized effort.

Want me to write that up as an actual `STRUCTURE` doc against the repo (same pattern as the Wheel/Pulse docs), or is this enough to work from directly?

**[ASSISTANT]**

1	# ADR-Gated Radial Delivery (AGRD) v1.0
     2	
     3	> **Status:** Draft v1.0 · **Supersedes:** Radial Incremental SDLC (Kimi v0.1) + ADR-Gated Continuous Architecture on Shape Up (Z.ai v0.1) + AGRD v0.1 (Kimi v0.2)
     4	> **Related:** `Architecture/INDEX.md` §5, `Architecture/OPEN-DECISIONS.md`, `Architecture/CrossCutting/STRUCTURE-01-Wheel.md`
     5	> **Decision required:** ADR-014 to ratify this model before Increment 1 begins
     6	
     7	---
     8	
     9	## 1. The two-layer truth
    10	
    11	Two models were proposed. Each captured half the truth:
    12	
    13	| Model | Captured | Missed |
    14	|---|---|---|
    15	| **Radial Incremental** (Kimi) | Wheel rings as delivery roadmap; Ring Lock gates; dependency-DAG-aware sequencing; tangential team flow | No ADR/lint integration; static architecture view; no cooldown; security at week 26 |
    16	| **ADR-Gated Continuous Architecture** (Z.ai) | ADRs as unit of work; lint as merge gate; doc-first culture; lightweight rituals; Open Decisions as backlog | Bets not mapped to rings; Shape Up "appetite" collides with DAG; security timing silent |
    17	
    18	**AGRD v1.0 unifies them in two layers:**
    19	
    20	```
    21	Layer 1 (Macro): Radial Incremental — the skeleton
    22	  → Rings are the roadmap. DAG enforces build order.
    23	  → One increment per ring. One bet per increment.
    24	
    25	Layer 2 (Micro): ADR-Gated — the nervous system
    26	  → Inside each increment: ADR queue, lint gates, PR flow, rituals.
    27	  → Governance lives inside the ring, not above it.
    28	```
    29	
    30	---
    31	
    32	## 2. Core principle: Architecture is the backlog
    33	
    34	The backlog is not a list of features. It is the canonical architecture itself:
    35	
    36	```
    37	Backlog = OPEN-DECISIONS.md        (decisions to make)
    38	        + STRUCTURE-XX gaps        (docs to write)
    39	        + Blueprint stubs          (specs to promote)
    40	        + ADR queue                (choices to ratify)
    41	```
    42	
    43	Code is a *derivative* of the backlog. A PR without an ADR reference is a **logical failure** — you cannot implement what has not been decided.
    44	
    45	---
    46	
    47	## 3. The delivery unit: Ring Bet
    48	
    49	A **Ring Bet** is a scoped commitment to deliver one Wheel ring to the fidelity bar defined in `AUTHORING_GUIDE.md`.
    50	
    51	### 3.1 Ring Bet map
    52	
    53	| Bet | Ring | Scope | Weeks | Entry Gate | Exit Gate (Ring Lock) |
    54	|-----|------|-------|-------|------------|----------------------|
    55	| 1 | **Core** | CORE-02→06, CORE-08→10, CORE-18 (booting kernel) | 6 | ADR-002 (PSR-11 container scope) accepted | `GET /healthz` returns 200; all Core blueprints meet fidelity bar; `run.php --ring=core` passes |
    56	| 2 | **Hub Critical** | HUB-01, HUB-02, HUB-04, HUB-05, HUB-08, HUB-20, HUB-21 | 8 | Bet 1 Ring Lock + ADR-004 (tier enforcement DAG) accepted | All critical Hub services register, boot, report healthy via HUB-15; cross-Hub Pulse contract tests pass |
    57	| 3 | **Hub Full** | Remaining HUB-03, HUB-06, HUB-07, HUB-09→19, HUB-22→30 | 7 | Bet 2 Ring Lock | All 30 Hub services pass CI; `run.php --ring=hub` zero findings |
    58	| 4 | **Thick Spokes (a)** | ISPOKE-01..15 (original set) | 6 | Bet 3 Ring Lock + ADR-005 (SuperPHP) accepted | Admin panel operational; tangential flow (ISPOKE→ISPOKE via HUB-08) tested |
    59	| 5 | **Thick Spokes (b)** | ISPOKE-16..25 (promoted set) | 6 | Bet 4 Ring Lock | All 25 ISPOKEs pass CI; workflow engine (ISPOKE-23) executes dormant Pulses |
    60	| 6 | **Inner Rim** | BRIDGE-01 hardening — Zero-Exposure enforcement, penetration policy verification | 3 | Bet 5 Ring Lock + ADR-003 (ES256 JWT) accepted | Zero-Exposure Test passes: anon write rejected at depth 3; tenant lane isolation enforced; every rejection audited |
    61	| 7 | **Thin Spokes + Outer Rim** | ESPOKE-01..15 + DEPLOY-01..04 | 8 | Bet 6 Ring Lock | Public API load test passes (STRUCTURE-09 targets); 15 ESPOKEs serve traffic; promotion pipeline (DEPLOY-04) deploys dev→staging→prod |
    62	| 8 | **Hardening** | Chaos engineering, security audit, GDPR compliance, performance baseline, rollback verification | 4 | Bet 7 Ring Lock | Penetration test clean; audit chain integrity verified; RTO/RPO targets met; rollback to Bet 7 state demonstrated |
    63	
    64	**Total: 48 weeks of bets + 7 weeks of cooldown = 55 weeks.**
    65	
    66	### 3.2 Why 6 weeks (±2) per ring
    67	
    68	- **Shorter:** You can't deliver a ring with fidelity. The `AUTHORING_GUIDE.md` bar requires interface contracts, benchmark methodology, CI verification criteria, and dependency audit.
    69	- **Longer:** The team loses momentum. A 3-person team needs a meaningful demo every 6–8 weeks.
    70	- **6 weeks is the appetite, not the estimate.** If a ring isn't done in its allotted weeks, the bet is **killed** or **descoped** — never extended. This prevents the "one more week" death spiral.
    71	- **Exceptions:** Bet 2 (Hub Critical) gets 8 weeks because 7 critical services with cross-dependencies need integration time. Bet 6 (Inner Rim) gets only 3 weeks because security was **moved left** — see §4.1.
    72	
    73	### 3.3 Cooldowns: Mandatory 1-week between bets
    74	
    75	48 weeks straight with 3 people → burnout. Between every bet: **1-week cooldown**.
    76	
    77	Cooldown work:
    78	- Update `INDEX.md` §2 if any IDs shifted
    79	- Reconcile `INCONSISTENCIES.md` — new contradictions found during implementation
    80	- Write missing STRUCTURE-XX sections
    81	- Pay down technical debt inside the locked ring (refactoring is safe — interface frozen)
    82	- Archive obsolete docs from `Analysis_Critiques_Rewrites/`
    83	- Plan next bet
    84	
    85	Cooldown is **not vacation**. It is why the canonical tree stays coherent while code accelerates.
    86	
    87	---
    88	
    89	## 4. The six fixes that make this work
    90	
    91	### Fix 1: Security moves left (non-negotiable)
    92	
    93	**The flaw:** BRIDGE-01 at weeks 26–32 means 26 weeks of code without security enforcement.
    94	
    95	**The fix:** BRIDGE-01's facets are built **incrementally across all rings**, not as a late phase:
    96	
    97	| Increment | Security deliverable | Scope |
    98	|-----------|---------------------|-------|
    99	| 1 (Core) | Authz facet **stub** | Every Core crossing gets a scope slot, even if `scope: internal` |
   100	| 2 (Hub Critical) | Authz facet **active** | JWT validation, scope extraction, tenant lane assignment live |
   101	| 3 (Hub Full) | Audit facet **active** | Every Hub crossing logged to HUB-06; `HUB-06` operational |
   102	| 4–5 (Thick Spokes) | ISPOKE audit **enforced** | Every ISPOKE crossing logged; tangential flow re-checks scope |
   103	| 6 (Inner Rim) | Zero-Exposure **enforcement** | Penetration policy rejects unauthorized depth; cache short-circuit on auth miss |
   104	
   105	Bet 6 shrinks from 6 weeks to **3 weeks** because the Vanguard is already built — it's now a **hardening and verification** phase, not build-from-scratch.
   106	
   107	### Fix 2: Two-tier interface freeze
   108	
   109	**The flaw:** SemVer freeze at week 6 (Core) is too rigid for greenfield. If Increment 3 reveals CORE-02 needs a new method, a hard freeze forces a major version bump or an ADR workaround.
   110	
   111	**The fix:**
   112	
   113	| Phase | Freeze type | Rule |
   114	|-------|-------------|------|
   115	| Increments 1–7 (build) | **Soft Freeze** | Breaking changes require an ADR + impact analysis on dependent rings. No version bump. The ADR documents the change and affected blueprints. |
   116	| Post-cutover (week 55+) | **Hard Freeze** | True SemVer. Breaking changes require major version + migration path. |
   117	
   118	This prevents rigidity during build while guaranteeing stability after launch.
   119	
   120	### Fix 3: OD resolution embedded in increments
   121	
   122	**The flaw:** The original plan never referenced the 6 Open Decisions.
   123	
   124	**The fix:** Each increment explicitly allocates capacity for resolving the ODs it unblocks:
   125	
   126	| Increment | ODs to resolve | Why |
   127	|---|---|---|
   128	| 1 (Core) | OD-06 (CORE-02 opcache baseline) | Blocks performance verification of Core |
   129	| 2 (Hub Critical) | OD-01 (HUB-31 real-time analytics) | Must decide before Hub interface soft-freezes |
   130	| 3 (Hub Full) | OD-05 (ISPOKE folder naming) | Must resolve before ISPOKE interfaces freeze |
   131	| 4 (Thick Spokes a) | OD-02 (post-quantum JWT) | Must resolve before authz contract hardens |
   132	| 5 (Thick Spokes b) | OD-03 (soft naming collision), OD-04 (exemplar labels) | Docs cleanup before public API exposure |
	< truncated lines 133-295 >
   296	| **Quarterly Reconciliation** | Every 3 months | 3 days | Canonical tree diff, archive old versions, renumber if needed, plan next 2 quarters |
   297	
   298	**Total ceremony overhead:** ~3.5 hours/week during bets, ~0 hours during cooldown (except Ring Lock). For a 3-person team, this is ~4% of available time.
   299	
   300	---
   301	
   302	## 9. Risk management: The "Kill the Bet" rule
   303	
   304	Shape Up's most important contribution is **appetite-based scoping**: if the work doesn't fit the appetite, you don't extend — you reduce scope or kill.
   305	
   306	### 9.1 Kill triggers (Bet Architecture Review, Week 3)
   307	
   308	| Trigger | Action |
   309	|---------|--------|
   310	| CORE-02 still a stub at Week 3 of Bet 1 | **Kill Bet 1.** Descope to "CORE-02 only." Extend Bet 1 to 6 weeks *just for CORE-02*. Everything else moves to Bet 2. |
   311	| HUB-04 cannot resolve OD-02 (post-quantum JWT) | **Kill the crypto facet.** Ship ES256 (ADR-003) as interim. Move post-quantum to Future Scale. |
   312	| ISPOKE-23 dormant Pulse format undecided | **Kill ISPOKE-23 from Bet 5.** Ship without sagas. Move C3 patch (dormant Pulses) to Future Scale. |
   313	| Zero-Exposure Test fails at Week 2 of Bet 6 | **Kill Bet 6.** Do not ship BRIDGE-01 without it. Extend by 1 week (the **only** exception — security is non-negotiable). |
   314	| Performance targets missed in Bet 7 | **Kill the ESPOKE that missed.** Ship the others. Move the failing ESPOKE to a follow-up bet. |
   315	
   316	### 9.2 Rollback strategy
   317	
   318	**The honest gap:** Neither original model addressed rollback.
   319	
   320	**The fix:** Every Ring Lock produces a **rollback artifact**:
   321	
   322	| Artifact | Contents | Stored |
   323	|----------|----------|--------|
   324	| Ring snapshot | Git tag + container image digest + DB migration version | GitHub Releases + container registry |
   325	| Pulse state | Serialized dormant Pulses, queue depth, cache warmness | `CORE-19` snapshot table |
   326	| Config state | `HUB-01` feature flags, `HUB-21` tenant config | Sealed secret backup |
   327	
   328	**Rollback rule:** If Increment N fails Ring Lock, roll back to Increment N-1 snapshot. The team has 48 hours to fix or kill the bet. No "fix forward" during Ring Lock — that way lies production incidents.
   329	
   330	---
   331	
   332	## 10. Performance verification per ring
   333	
   334	**The honest gap:** STRUCTURE-09 exists but neither model referenced it.
   335	
   336	**The fix:** Every Ring Lock includes performance benchmarks:
   337	
   338	| Bet | Performance target | Method |
   339	|-----|-------------------|--------|
   340	| 1 (Core) | DI container resolves 1,000 bindings in < 10ms | `phpunit --group performance` on GitHub Actions runner |
   341	| 2 (Hub Critical) | HUB-04 authenticates 1,000 JWTs/sec | `wrk` against stub endpoint |
   342	| 3 (Hub Full) | HUB-15 health check returns in < 50ms at 100 RPS | `k6` load test |
   343	| 4–5 (Thick Spokes) | ISPOKE-01 admin panel renders in < 200ms | Lighthouse CI |
   344	| 6 (Inner Rim) | BRIDGE-01 rejects unauthorized Pulse in < 5ms | `wrk` with invalid JWT |
   345	| 7 (Thin Spokes) | ESPOKE-01 CMS serves 1,000 concurrent users, p95 < 500ms | `k6` against staging |
   346	| 8 (Hardening) | Full system: 10,000 RPS, p99 < 1s | Load test against production-like cluster |
   347	
   348	Performance targets are **provisional until Bet 7**. Before Bet 7, they are "best effort, measured, not enforced." From Bet 7 onward, they are **blocking**.
   349	
   350	---
   351	
   352	## 11. Production operations: The 9th increment (ongoing)
   353	
   354	**The honest gap:** Increment 8 ends at deployment, but SRE/oncall/incident response (ISPOKE-25!) needs a lifecycle too.
   355	
   356	**The fix:** AGRD defines an ongoing **9th increment** — not a bet, but a perpetual operating model:
   357	
   358	| Activity | Owner | Cadence |
   359	|----------|-------|---------|
   360	| On-call rotation | Engineer C (rotating) | Weekly |
   361	| Incident response | ISPOKE-25 (Incident Response Console) | Event-driven |
   362	| Security patching | Engineer A | Monthly |
   363	| Performance regression | Engineer B | Weekly (automated alert) |
   364	| OD triage | Rotating chair | Weekly ADR Review |
   365	| Quarterly reconciliation | Team | Every 3 months |
   366	| Annual architecture review | Team + external reviewer | Every 12 months |
   367	
   368	This is documented in `Architecture/OPERATIONS.md` (to be written during Bet 8 cooldown).
   369	
   370	---
   371	
   372	## 12. Comparison: AGRD vs. alternatives
   373	
   374	| Criterion | AGRD | Scrum | Waterfall | Pure Shape Up |
   375	|-----------|------|-------|-----------|---------------|
   376	| **Maps to Wheel architecture** | ✅ Rings as bets | ❌ Sprints ignore rings | ❌ One big bang | ⚠️ Bets don't know rings |
   377	| **Leverages existing lint** | ✅ `run.php --ring` | ❌ Lint is "debt" | ❌ No lint | ⚠️ No arch lint |
   378	| **Handles dependency DAG** | ✅ Ring sequence | ❌ Sprints pretend parallel | ✅ Planned but rigid | ⚠️ Appetite ignores DAG |
   379	| **Doc-first culture** | ✅ ADR gates | ❌ Stories are code-centric | ✅ Docs frozen early | ⚠️ Pitch is product-centric |
   380	| **3-person team fit** | ✅ Light rituals | ❌ Ceremony-heavy | ❌ Too heavy | ✅ Light rituals |
   381	| **Open Decisions tracked** | ✅ OD queue = backlog | ❌ Backlog is features | ❌ Change requests | ⚠️ No decision tracking |
   382	| **Interface stability** | ✅ Soft → Hard Freeze | ❌ Refactoring sprints | ✅ Frozen too early | ⚠️ Scope changes per bet |
   383	| **Security timing** | ✅ Moved left (Fix 1) | ❌ Per-sprint | ❌ Late phase | ❌ Silent |
   384	| **Performance verification** | ✅ Per-ring benchmarks | ❌ Sprint retro | ❌ End-only | ⚠️ Optional |
   385	| **Rollback strategy** | ✅ Ring snapshots | ❌ Revert PR | ❌ Big bang rollback | ❌ Not addressed |
   386	| **Bus factor mitigation** | ✅ Primary + reviewer | ❌ Solo ownership | ❌ Handoff docs | ❌ Not addressed |
   387

   409	
   410	## 14. The one anti-pattern to watch for
   411	
   412	> **Don't let the linter become the architect.**
   413	
   414	The linter (`run.php`) enforces decisions already made. It does not make decisions. If the team finds itself editing canonical docs to satisfy the linter — rather than editing the linter to reflect canonical decisions — the model is inverted.
   415	
   416	**The ADR Review is where decisions are made. The linter is the notary. The Ring Lock is the seal.**
   417	
   418	---
   419	
   420	## 15. Open questions (for AGRD v1.1)
   421	
   422	1. **Bet overlap:** Can Bet N+1 planning start during Bet N's cooldown? *(Recommended: Yes, planning only. No code.)*
   423	2. **HUB-31 exception:** If OD-01 is accepted mid-Bet 2, does Hub become 31 components? *(Recommended: No. HUB-31 ships in a later bet or cooldown.)*
   424	3. **External contributors:** How does a PR from an external contributor work? *(Recommended: Must reference an accepted ADR and pass all gates. No exceptions.)*
   425	4. **P0 bug during cooldown:** Break cooldown? *(Recommended: Yes, with emergency ADR. Cooldown resumes after fix.)*
   426	5. **Annual review:** Should AGRD itself be reviewed annually? *(Recommended: Yes, at Quarterly Reconciliation in Q4.)*
   427	     1	# ADR-Gated Radial Delivery (AGRD) v2
     2	
     3	**Subtitle:** A Synthesized SDLC for the DGLab Wheel Architecture
     4	**Status:** Proposed
     5	**Supersedes:** ADR-013 (to be written — adopts this model as canonical SDLC)
     6	**Authors:** Synthesis of Z.ai (AGCA) + Kimi (Radial Incremental)
     7	
     8	---
     9	
    10	## 1. Executive Summary
    11	
    12	The DGLab Wheel architecture — 96 blueprints across 6 concentric rings (Core, Hub, Thick Spokes, Inner Rim, Thin Spokes, Outer Rim), strict tier isolation, a polyrepo strategy, a 244-line architecture linter, and 6 open decisions — does not fit cleanly into any textbook SDLC. This document defines **ADR-Gated Radial Delivery (AGRD) v2**, a synthesized model that uses Radial Incremental as the macro cadence and ADR-Gated Continuous Architecture as the micro cadence inside each ring.
    13	
    14	**The one-line model:** *The Wheel's rings are the roadmap. ADRs are the gates. 6-week bets are the cadence. The linter is the notary. The Ring Lock is the seal.*
    15	
    16	AGRD v2 incorporates five fixes beyond the original AGRD synthesis:
    17	
    18	1. **Security actually moves left** — BRIDGE-01's three facets are built incrementally across all bets, not bolted on at Bet 6.
    19	2. **Bet 7 splits into two** — Thin Spokes (application surface) and Outer Rim (deployment infra) have different risk profiles and shouldn't share a Ring Lock.
    20	3. **Bet 8 (Hardening) decomposes** into three 2-week sub-bets.
    21	4. **Cooldowns are 1 week, not 2** — arithmetic is corrected (original AGRD claimed "48 weeks + cooldowns" but actually totaled 62 weeks).
    22	5. **All 6 Open Decisions (ODs) are explicitly mapped** to specific bets where they must be resolved.
    23	
    24	**Honest timeline:** 52 weeks for full production readiness with proper security enforcement. 4 weeks over the optimistic 48-week target — the cost of building security left rather than bolting it on at week 26.
    25	
    26	---
    27	
    28	## 2. Why Standard SDLCs Fail Here
    29	
    30	| Model | Why It Breaks |
    31	|-------|---------------|
    32	| **Pure Waterfall** | 48+ weeks without working software is fatal for a greenfield framework. You'd discover CORE-02 is wrong in month 11. |
    33	| **Pure Scrum** | Sprints assume parallelizable stories. You can't put HUB-04 in Sprint 3 when CORE-02 won't land until Sprint 6. The dependency DAG makes sprint planning a lie. |
    34	| **Pure Kanban** | No milestone structure. A 3-person team would drift without the ring boundaries forcing integration. |
    35	| **Spiral** | Overkill. Your risk profile is architectural (can we build the DI container?), not market (will users buy this?). |
    36	| **V-Model** | Good for verification but too rigid for iterative interface refinement between rings. |
    37	| **Shape Up (pure)** | The "appetite" concept collides with the DAG — you can't appetite past CORE-02 being a stub. |
    38	| **SAFe** | Ceremony overhead (PI planning, ART sync) is wildly disproportionate to your repo count. |
    39	| **DocOps alone** | Captures the doc-first principle but has no delivery cadence, so OD backlog grows unbounded. |
    40	
    41	---
    42	
    43	## 3. What Each Prior Model Captured
    44	
    45	| Model | Got Right | Got Wrong |
    46	|-------|-----------|-----------|
    47	| **Radial Incremental** (Kimi) | Wheel rings as delivery roadmap; Ring Lock gates freeze interfaces; dependency-DAG-aware sequencing; 3-person team optimization | Didn't leverage existing ADR/lint infrastructure; treated architecture as static; no cooldown mechanism; BRIDGE-01 at week 26 = security blind spot |
    48	| **ADR-Gated Continuous Architecture** (Z.ai) | ADRs as the unit of work; `run.php` as the merge gate; doc-first culture; lightweight rituals; Open Decisions as backlog | Didn't map bets to Wheel rings; Shape Up's "appetite" collides with the DAG; under-emphasized the ring structure; ignored build order entirely |
    49	
    50	---
    51	
    52	## 4. The AGRD v2 Model
    53	
    54	### 4.1 Core Principles
    55	
    56	1. **Rings are the roadmap.** Each Wheel ring maps to one or more "bets" of 6 weeks. The dependency DAG enforces inside-out sequencing.
    57	2. **ADRs are the gates.** Every PR references an ADR (or marks itself "ADR-exempt: implementation-only"). The ADR Review is where decisions are made.
    58	3. **The linter is the notary, not the architect.** `run.php` enforces decisions; it doesn't make them. If the team edits canonical docs to satisfy the linter rather than the reverse, the model has inverted.
    59	4. **Pulse-Driven Demos.** Every Ring Lock demo is a live Pulse — `GET /healthz` → depth 6 → `200 OK`. No PowerPoint. Live Pulses or it didn't happen.
    60	5. **Tangential team flow.** 3 engineers work parallel *within* a ring (tangential), never *across* rings (radial). All 3 swarm CORE-02 in week 1; split into facets once it lands.
    61	6. **Security moves left.** BRIDGE-01's Authz, Audit, and Zero-Exposure facets are built incrementally across all bets — not bolted on at week 26.
    62	7. **Soft Freeze during build, Hard Freeze post-cutover.** Ring Lock applies a Soft Freeze (ADR-gated changes allowed) for Bets 1–8, transitioning to Hard Freeze (true SemVer) after production cutover.
    63	
    64	### 4.2 The Unified Cadence
    65	
    66	```
    67	Layer 1 (Macro): Radial Incremental
    68	  Bet 1 (Core)              Weeks 1–6   + 1 cooldown
    69	  Bet 2 (Hub Critical)      Weeks 8–13  + 1 cooldown
    70	  Bet 3 (Hub Full)          Weeks 15–20 + 1 cooldown
    71	  Bet 4 (Thick Spokes a)    Weeks 22–27 + 1 cooldown
    72	  Bet 5 (Thick Spokes b)    Weeks 29–34 + 1 cooldown
    73	  Bet 6 (Inner Rim)         Weeks 36–39 + parallel
    74	  Bet 7 (Thin Spokes)       Weeks 36–45
    75	  Bet 8 (Outer Rim)         Weeks 35–45 + 1 cooldown
    76	  Bet 9 (Hardening)         Weeks 47–52
    77	
    78	Layer 2 (Micro, inside each bet):
    79	  Weekly ADR review (30 min) — triage ODs for this ring
    80	  Continuous PR flow — lint-gated, ADR-referenced
    81	  Pulse-Driven Demo at Ring Lock — live Pulse, no slides
    82	  Soft Freeze on interface — ADR-gated changes allowed
    83	  Cross-repo Pulse contract tests — mandatory at Ring Lock
    84	  Canonical doc version bump — STRUCTURE-XX vX.Y per ring
    85	```
    86	
    87	### 4.3 The One Rule That Prevents Both Failure Modes
    88	
    89	> **Don't let the linter become the architect.** The ADR Review is where decisions are made. The linter is the notary. The Ring Lock is the seal.
    90	
    91	This prevents:
    92	- Radial Incremental's risk: architecture drifting because there's no decision-making ritual.
    93	- AGCA's risk: team editing canonical docs to satisfy the linter rather than the reverse.
    94	
    95	---
    96

    99	| Bet | Ring | Build Weeks | ODs Resolved | BRIDGE-01 Facet Status | Ring Lock Demo |
   100	|-----|------|-------------|--------------|------------------------|----------------|

   102	| — | cooldown | 7 | — | — | — |
   103	| 2 | **Hub Critical** | 8–13 | — | Authz *active* — JWT validation, scope extraction | `POST /admin/login` → depth 5 → JWT minted |
   104	| — | cooldown | 14 | — | — | — |

   118	
   119	### 5.1 Why Bets 6, 7, 8 Overlap
   120	
   121	Inner Rim hardening (Bet 6), Thin Spokes build (Bet 7), and Outer Rim pipeline (Bet 8) all run in parallel once Core + Hub + Thick Spokes are stable:
   122	
   123	- Bet 8 (Outer Rim) can start at week 35 because it depends on Core + Hub, not Thin Spokes.
   124	- Bet 6 (Inner Rim) is a 3-week hardening bet, not a 6-week build — the facets were built incrementally.
   125	- Bet 7 (Thin Spokes) is the longest of the three and gates the final integration.

   129	## 6. Bet Details
   130	
   131	### Bet 1: Core Ring (Weeks 1–6)
   132	
   133	**Goal:** A booting kernel that can route `GET /healthz` and return `200 OK`.
	< truncated lines 134-403 >
   404	| **Requirements** | Fixed (blueprints done) | Evolving | Fixed | Evolving |
   405	| **Delivery** | Ring-by-ring working software | Sprint increments | Big bang | Bet-driven |
   406	| **Dependencies** | Enforced by architecture + DAG | Ignored | Planned | Ignored |
   407	| **Team size fit** | 3–5 engineers | 5–9 | Any | 3–5 |

   433	## 14. Still Missing (Honest Gaps)
   434	
   435	AGRD v2 is the closest synthesis yet, but these gaps remain:
   436	
   437	1. **Bus factor mitigation beyond facet rotation.** What if an engineer leaves mid-bet? Suggest: each blueprint has a primary and a reviewer from a different facet, enforced by PR template. Rotation every 2 bets is a starting point, not a complete solution.
   438	
   439	2. **Post-cutover ops SDLC.** ISPOKE-24 (Backup) and ISPOKE-25 (Incident Response) need an ongoing lifecycle, not a one-time bet. This is a 10th "increment" that's permanent. Define it as a separate ops SDLC with oncall rotations, postmortem templates, and quarterly chaos exercises.
   440	
   441	3. **Performance verification per ring.** STRUCTURE-09 exists but isn't referenced in Ring Lock criteria. Each Ring Lock should include performance benchmarks from STRUCTURE-09, not just functional demos. Add a "perf gate" to the Ring Lock Ceremony.
   442	
   443	4. **Cross-training plan.** 3 people, 96 blueprints. If Engineer A (Identity facet) is sick during Bet 6 (Inner Rim), can B or C cover? Explicit cross-training: each engineer spends 2 hours/week pairing with another facet's primary.
   444	
   445	5. **Tooling for cross-repo Pulse contract tests.** AGRD v2 mandates these at every Ring Lock, but the tooling doesn't exist yet. Build it in Bet 1 as part of CORE-18 (Kernel) — a `pulse-contract-test` CLI that verifies 6-tuple consistency across polyrepos.
   446	
   447	6. **Quarterly canonical reconciliation.** The Aug 5 v0.1 → v0.4 jump was healthy but ad hoc. Formalize a quarterly "canonical reconciliation week" where the team diffs the canonical tree, archives obsolete versions, and renumbers if needed.
   448	
   449	---
   450	
   451	## 15. Bottom Line
   452	
   453	**Use AGRD v2 because:**
   454	
   455	1. The architecture *is* the plan — 96 blueprints with a strict DAG.
   456	2. The Wheel model *is* the delivery model — rings must be built inside-out.
   457	3. The team size *is* the constraint — 3 people need flow, not ceremony.
   458	4. The verification *is* the definition of done — every blueprint already has CI criteria.
   459	5. The ODs *are* the backlog — no separate "feature backlog" competes with architectural decisions.
   460	6. The linter *is* the notary — `run.php` enforces decisions; the ADR Review makes them.
   461	
   462	**Start Monday with all 3 engineers on CORE-02. Ring Lock in 6 weeks. Repeat.**
   463	
   464	**Honest timeline: 52 weeks, not 48.** The 4-week delta is the cost of building security left rather than bolting it on at week 26. Better to acknowledge it now than discover it at week 40 with no security enforcement and no deployment pipeline.
   465	
   466	---
   467	
   468	## Appendix A: ADR-013 Draft (Adopt AGRD v2)
   469	
   470	```markdown
   471	# ADR-013: Adopt ADR-Gated Radial Delivery (AGRD) v2 as Canonical SDLC
   472	
   473	**Status:** Proposed
   474	**Date:** 【____/____/____】
   475	**Supersedes:** — (first canonical SDLC ADR)
   476	
   477	## Context
   478	
   479	The DGLab Wheel architecture (v0.4 canonical) defines 96 blueprints across 6 concentric rings with strict tier isolation, a polyrepo strategy, and a 244-line architecture linter. No textbook SDLC fits cleanly: Waterfall cannot tolerate the iterative interface refinement between rings; Scrum's sprint model ignores the dependency DAG; Shape Up's "appetite" concept collides with the DAG.
   480	
   481	Two prior models were proposed:
   482	- **Radial Incremental** (Kimi) — uses rings as the roadmap but ignores existing ADR/lint infrastructure and places BRIDGE-01 at week 26 (security blind spot).
   483	- **ADR-Gated Continuous Architecture** (Z.ai) — uses ADRs as the unit of work but ignores build order and ring structure.
   484	
   485	## Decision
   486	
   487	Adopt **ADR-Gated Radial Delivery (AGRD) v2** as the canonical SDLC, as defined in `Architecture/CrossCutting/SDLC-AGRD.md`.
   488	
   489	The model has two layers:
   490	- **Macro:** Radial Incremental — 9 ring bets across 52 weeks.
   491	- **Micro:** ADR-Gated — weekly ADR reviews, lint-gated PRs, Pulse contract tests, Soft-Freeze interfaces.
   492	
   493	Key principles:
   494	1. Rings are the roadmap; ADRs are the gates; the linter is the notary; the Ring Lock is the seal.
   495	2. Security moves left — BRIDGE-01 facets built incrementally across all bets.
   496	3. Soft Freeze during build; Hard Freeze post-cutover.
   497	4. ODs are the only backlog; each maps to a target bet.
   498	
   499	## Consequences
   500	
   501	**Positive:**
   502	- Build order respects the DAG.
   503	- Decision governance uses existing ADR/lint infrastructure.
   504	- Security is enforced from Bet 1, not bolted on at Bet 6.
   505	- 52-week honest timeline (4 weeks over optimistic 48).
   506	
   507	**Negative:**
   508	- 52 weeks exceeds the original 48-week target by 4.
   509	- Requires a `--ring` flag in `run.php` (tooling work in Bet 1).
   510	- Requires cross-repo Pulse contract test tooling (also Bet 1).
   511	- Facet rotation every 2 bets adds coordination overhead.
   512	
   513	**Neutral:**
   514	- This ADR locks in the SDLC; future changes require ADR-amend.
   515	```
   516	
   517	---
   518	
   519	## Appendix B: Glossary
   520	
   521	| Term | Definition |
   522	|------|------------|
   523	| **AGRD** | ADR-Gated Radial Delivery — the synthesized SDLC defined in this document |
   524	| **Ring Bet** | A 6-week commitment to build one Wheel ring (or a portion of it) |
   525	| **Ring Lock** | The ceremony at the end of a bet: demo + lint + ADR ratify + interface freeze |
   526	| **Ring Lock Ceremony** | The formal 5-step process: Pulse demo, lint verification, contract tests, ADR ratification, Soft-Freeze |
   527	| **Soft Freeze** | Interface changes allowed with an ADR; no SemVer version bump (during Bets 1–8) |
   528	| **Hard Freeze** | True SemVer enforcement; breaking changes require major version + migration (post-cutover) |
   529	| **Pulse-Driven Demo** | A live Pulse traverses the ring and returns — no slides |
   530	| **Tangential Flow** | Engineers work parallel within a ring, never across rings |
   531	| **Dormant Pulse** | A long-running task that serializes state, context-switches, and resumes |
   532	| **OD** | Open Decision — an unresolved architectural question in `OPEN-DECISIONS.md` |
   533	| **ADR** | Architecture Decision Record — the unit of architectural change |
   534	| **BRIDGE-01** | The Inner Rim checkpoint (Vanguard) — enforces Zero-Exposure |
   535	| **`run.php`** | The 244-line architecture linter — the notary, not the architect |
   536	Different genre entirely — this is an SDLC/delivery-methodology synthesis, not architecture critique. Before I give you my take, let me actually verify the checkable claims against the real repo state, since that's been the pattern all session and this document leans heavily on specific ADR numbers, line counts, and component references that are either right or aren't.{"returncode":0,"stdout":"=== ADR directory listing ===\nADR-001-polyrepo-vs-monorepo.md\nADR-002-psr11-container-scope.md\nADR-003-es256-jwt-signing.md\nADR-004-tier-enforcement-dag.md\nADR-005-superphp-vs-blade-twig.md\nADR-006-redis-over-memcached.md\nADR-007-postgresql-over-mysql.md\nADR-008-argon2id-password-hashing.md\nADR-009-ulid-over-uuid.md\nADR-010-opcache-preload-strategy.md\nADR-011-hub-31-real-time-analytics.md\nADR-013-mysql-primary-datastore.md\n\n=== CrossCutting directory listing (checking for STRUCTURE-09) ===\nGLOSSARY.md\nOBSERVABILITY.md\nSTRUCTURE-01-Wheel.md\nSTRUCTURE-02-Pulse.md\nSTRUCTURE-03-Security.md\nSTRUCTURE-04-Events.md\nSTRUCTURE-05-Persistence.md\nSTRUCTURE-06-Boot.md\nSTRUCTURE-07-Testing.md\nSTRUCTURE-08-Deployment.md\nSTRUCTURE-09-Performance.md\nTHREAT_MODEL.md\n\n=== run.php actual line count ===\n244\n\n=== ISPOKE-23 (claimed to be workflow/dormant-Pulse engine) ===\n# PHASE ISPOKE-23: Sovereign Role Play (Simulation)\n\n## Tier\nInternal Spoke (Staff-only — VPN/bastion)\n\n## Component Name\nSovereign Role Play — `SovereignStack\\Internal\\RolePlay`. Sandbox for testing RBAC configurations before\ndeployment: \"what-if\" analysis of permission changes, role preview, conflict detection.\n\n## Description\nISPOKE-23 lets administrators preview the effect of a proposed RBAC change (new role, permission grant,\nrole merge) against a **simulated** subject, without touching live policy in **HUB-05 (Sovereign\nGuardian)**. It clones the current policy graph, applies the candidate change in the clone, and reports\nthe resulting effective permissions plus any conflicts (e.g. a Permission X granted to a role that is\ndenied elsewhere, or a separation-of-duties violation). It is read-only against production and writes\nonly to its own simulation store.\n\n## Build Status\n✅ **Documented — ready for implementation.**\n\n","stderr":""}Genuinely solid synthesis in places — I checked the specific, falsifiable claims rather than just reading it as prose, and most of it holds up: 96 blueprints is exactly right (20 Core + 30 Hub + 25 Internal + 15 External + 1 Bridge + 5 Deploy, correctly excluding `HUB-31` since it's still pending), the "244-line linter" claim is exact, and `STRUCTURE-09-Performance.md` is real. That's not sloppy work.

Two things don't hold up, though, and one is a live collision that matters for the migration plan specifically.

**`ADR-013` is already taken — by the MySQL pivot.** AGRD v2's header says *"Supersedes: ADR-013 (to be written — adopts this model as canonical SDLC)"*, and its Day-8 migration step and full Appendix A draft both title themselves `ADR-013: Adopt AGRD v2...`. But `ADR-013-mysql-primary-datastore.md` already exists and was accepted this morning — that's the commit I checked with you a few turns ago. This reads like AGRD v2 was finalized without a fresh pull of the ADR directory. AGRD v1 actually got this right (it correctly used `ADR-014` for the same adoption decision) — v2 regressed on its own predecessor's numbering. Easy fix: renumber v2's adoption ADR to `ADR-014` (there's also a gap at `ADR-012` worth checking whether that's reserved or just skipped before you claim either number).

**`ISPOKE-23` isn't a workflow/dormant-Pulse engine — it's RBAC simulation.** Both versions lean on this: v1's kill-trigger table says *"ISPOKE-23 dormant Pulse format undecided → Kill ISPOKE-23 from Bet 5. Ship without sagas,"* and v2's glossary defines "Dormant Pulse" (a long-running task that serializes, context-switches, and resumes) as if `ISPOKE-23` implements it. The actual `ISPOKE-23` is **"Sovereign Role Play"** — a what-if sandbox for previewing RBAC changes against a cloned policy graph before touching live `HUB-05`. Nothing to do with sagas or long-running task resumption. This means "dormant Pulses" is a real concept with **no backing component at all** — the same situation `HUB-31` was in before it got an honest `pending` status via `ADR-011`. Retrofitting it onto `ISPOKE-23` (or killing "ISPOKE-23" thinking that kills the saga feature) doesn't actually resolve anything; it just hides an open decision behind a component that does something else entirely. If dormant Pulses are wanted, that's a 7th Open Decision, not a kill-trigger on existing scope.

One structural tension worth naming, not just a factual bug: **Core Principle 5** ("engineers work parallel within a ring, never across rings") sits awkwardly next to **§5.1**, which has Bets 6 (Inner Rim), 7 (Thin Spokes), and 8 (Outer Rim) running concurrently across weeks 35–45. At the individual level that's fine — one engineer per bet stays tangential to their own ring — but at the team level you'd have Outer Rim (public-facing, highest blast radius) under active build in the same weeks Inner Rim's Zero-Exposure enforcement is still being *hardened*, not yet locked. The security-moves-left fixes make this defensible (Authz/Audit are already active by week 34), but the document should say so explicitly rather than let the "never across rings" principle imply a guarantee the overlap section quietly doesn't provide.

Want me to draft the two concrete fixes (renumber the adoption ADR, and a short `OD-07: Dormant Pulse / saga execution model` entry for `OPEN-DECISIONS.md` instead of the `ISPOKE-23` misattribution), or go deeper on the methodology itself first?
