**[ASSISTANT]**

1	# ADR-Gated Radial Delivery (AGRD) v1.0
     2	
     3	> **Status:** Draft v1.0 · **Supersedes:** Radial Incremental SDLC (Kimi v0.1) + ADR-Gated Continuous Architecture on Shape Up (Z.ai v0.1) + AGRD v0.1 (Kimi v0.2)
     4	> **Related:** `Architecture/INDEX.md` §5, `Architecture/OPEN-DECISIONS.md`, `Architecture/CrossCutting/STRUCTURE-01-Wheel.md`
     5	> **Decision required:** ADR-014 to ratify this model before Increment 1 begins
     6	
     7	---
     8	
     9	## 1. The two-layer truth
    10	
    11	Two models were proposed. Each captured half the truth:
    12	
    13	| Model | Captured | Missed |
    14	|---|---|---|
    15	| **Radial Incremental** (Kimi) | Wheel rings as delivery roadmap; Ring Lock gates; dependency-DAG-aware sequencing; tangential team flow | No ADR/lint integration; static architecture view; no cooldown; security at week 26 |
    16	| **ADR-Gated Continuous Architecture** (Z.ai) | ADRs as unit of work; lint as merge gate; doc-first culture; lightweight rituals; Open Decisions as backlog | Bets not mapped to rings; Shape Up "appetite" collides with DAG; security timing silent |
    17	
    18	**AGRD v1.0 unifies them in two layers:**
    19	
    20	```
    21	Layer 1 (Macro): Radial Incremental — the skeleton
    22	  → Rings are the roadmap. DAG enforces build order.
    23	  → One increment per ring. One bet per increment.
    24	
    25	Layer 2 (Micro): ADR-Gated — the nervous system
    26	  → Inside each increment: ADR queue, lint gates, PR flow, rituals.
    27	  → Governance lives inside the ring, not above it.
    28	```
    29	
    30	---
    31	
    32	## 2. Core principle: Architecture is the backlog
    33	
    34	The backlog is not a list of features. It is the canonical architecture itself:
    35	
    36	```
    37	Backlog = OPEN-DECISIONS.md        (decisions to make)
    38	        + STRUCTURE-XX gaps        (docs to write)
    39	        + Blueprint stubs          (specs to promote)
    40	        + ADR queue                (choices to ratify)
    41	```
    42	
    43	Code is a *derivative* of the backlog. A PR without an ADR reference is a **logical failure** — you cannot implement what has not been decided.
    44	
    45	---
    46	
    47	## 3. The delivery unit: Ring Bet
    48	
    49	A **Ring Bet** is a scoped commitment to deliver one Wheel ring to the fidelity bar defined in `AUTHORING_GUIDE.md`.
    50	
    51	### 3.1 Ring Bet map
    52	
    53	| Bet | Ring | Scope | Weeks | Entry Gate | Exit Gate (Ring Lock) |
    54	|-----|------|-------|-------|------------|----------------------|
    55	| 1 | **Core** | CORE-02→06, CORE-08→10, CORE-18 (booting kernel) | 6 | ADR-002 (PSR-11 container scope) accepted | `GET /healthz` returns 200; all Core blueprints meet fidelity bar; `run.php --ring=core` passes |
    56	| 2 | **Hub Critical** | HUB-01, HUB-02, HUB-04, HUB-05, HUB-08, HUB-20, HUB-21 | 8 | Bet 1 Ring Lock + ADR-004 (tier enforcement DAG) accepted | All critical Hub services register, boot, report healthy via HUB-15; cross-Hub Pulse contract tests pass |
    57	| 3 | **Hub Full** | Remaining HUB-03, HUB-06, HUB-07, HUB-09→19, HUB-22→30 | 7 | Bet 2 Ring Lock | All 30 Hub services pass CI; `run.php --ring=hub` zero findings |
    58	| 4 | **Thick Spokes (a)** | ISPOKE-01..15 (original set) | 6 | Bet 3 Ring Lock + ADR-005 (SuperPHP) accepted | Admin panel operational; tangential flow (ISPOKE→ISPOKE via HUB-08) tested |
    59	| 5 | **Thick Spokes (b)** | ISPOKE-16..25 (promoted set) | 6 | Bet 4 Ring Lock | All 25 ISPOKEs pass CI; workflow engine (ISPOKE-23) executes dormant Pulses |
    60	| 6 | **Inner Rim** | BRIDGE-01 hardening — Zero-Exposure enforcement, penetration policy verification | 3 | Bet 5 Ring Lock + ADR-003 (ES256 JWT) accepted | Zero-Exposure Test passes: anon write rejected at depth 3; tenant lane isolation enforced; every rejection audited |
    61	| 7 | **Thin Spokes + Outer Rim** | ESPOKE-01..15 + DEPLOY-01..04 | 8 | Bet 6 Ring Lock | Public API load test passes (STRUCTURE-09 targets); 15 ESPOKEs serve traffic; promotion pipeline (DEPLOY-04) deploys dev→staging→prod |
    62	| 8 | **Hardening** | Chaos engineering, security audit, GDPR compliance, performance baseline, rollback verification | 4 | Bet 7 Ring Lock | Penetration test clean; audit chain integrity verified; RTO/RPO targets met; rollback to Bet 7 state demonstrated |
    63	
    64	**Total: 48 weeks of bets + 7 weeks of cooldown = 55 weeks.**
    65	
    66	### 3.2 Why 6 weeks (±2) per ring
    67	
    68	- **Shorter:** You can't deliver a ring with fidelity. The `AUTHORING_GUIDE.md` bar requires interface contracts, benchmark methodology, CI verification criteria, and dependency audit.
    69	- **Longer:** The team loses momentum. A 3-person team needs a meaningful demo every 6–8 weeks.
    70	- **6 weeks is the appetite, not the estimate.** If a ring isn't done in its allotted weeks, the bet is **killed** or **descoped** — never extended. This prevents the "one more week" death spiral.
    71	- **Exceptions:** Bet 2 (Hub Critical) gets 8 weeks because 7 critical services with cross-dependencies need integration time. Bet 6 (Inner Rim) gets only 3 weeks because security was **moved left** — see §4.1.
    72	
    73	### 3.3 Cooldowns: Mandatory 1-week between bets
    74	
    75	48 weeks straight with 3 people → burnout. Between every bet: **1-week cooldown**.
    76	
    77	Cooldown work:
    78	- Update `INDEX.md` §2 if any IDs shifted
    79	- Reconcile `INCONSISTENCIES.md` — new contradictions found during implementation
    80	- Write missing STRUCTURE-XX sections
    81	- Pay down technical debt inside the locked ring (refactoring is safe — interface frozen)
    82	- Archive obsolete docs from `Analysis_Critiques_Rewrites/`
    83	- Plan next bet
    84	
    85	Cooldown is **not vacation**. It is why the canonical tree stays coherent while code accelerates.
    86	
    87	---
    88	
    89	## 4. The six fixes that make this work
    90	
    91	### Fix 1: Security moves left (non-negotiable)
    92	
    93	**The flaw:** BRIDGE-01 at weeks 26–32 means 26 weeks of code without security enforcement.
    94	
    95	**The fix:** BRIDGE-01's facets are built **incrementally across all rings**, not as a late phase:
    96	
    97	| Increment | Security deliverable | Scope |
    98	|-----------|---------------------|-------|
    99	| 1 (Core) | Authz facet **stub** | Every Core crossing gets a scope slot, even if `scope: internal` |
   100	| 2 (Hub Critical) | Authz facet **active** | JWT validation, scope extraction, tenant lane assignment live |
   101	| 3 (Hub Full) | Audit facet **active** | Every Hub crossing logged to HUB-06; `HUB-06` operational |
   102	| 4–5 (Thick Spokes) | ISPOKE audit **enforced** | Every ISPOKE crossing logged; tangential flow re-checks scope |
   103	| 6 (Inner Rim) | Zero-Exposure **enforcement** | Penetration policy rejects unauthorized depth; cache short-circuit on auth miss |
   104	
   105	Bet 6 shrinks from 6 weeks to **3 weeks** because the Vanguard is already built — it's now a **hardening and verification** phase, not build-from-scratch.
   106	
   107	### Fix 2: Two-tier interface freeze
   108	
   109	**The flaw:** SemVer freeze at week 6 (Core) is too rigid for greenfield. If Increment 3 reveals CORE-02 needs a new method, a hard freeze forces a major version bump or an ADR workaround.
   110	
   111	**The fix:**
   112	
   113	| Phase | Freeze type | Rule |
   114	|-------|-------------|------|
   115	| Increments 1–7 (build) | **Soft Freeze** | Breaking changes require an ADR + impact analysis on dependent rings. No version bump. The ADR documents the change and affected blueprints. |
   116	| Post-cutover (week 55+) | **Hard Freeze** | True SemVer. Breaking changes require major version + migration path. |
   117	
   118	This prevents rigidity during build while guaranteeing stability after launch.
   119	
   120	### Fix 3: OD resolution embedded in increments
   121	
   122	**The flaw:** The original plan never referenced the 6 Open Decisions.
   123	
   124	**The fix:** Each increment explicitly allocates capacity for resolving the ODs it unblocks:
   125	
   126	| Increment | ODs to resolve | Why |
   127	|---|---|---|
   128	| 1 (Core) | OD-06 (CORE-02 opcache baseline) | Blocks performance verification of Core |
   129	| 2 (Hub Critical) | OD-01 (HUB-31 real-time analytics) | Must decide before Hub interface soft-freezes |
   130	| 3 (Hub Full) | OD-05 (ISPOKE folder naming) | Must resolve before ISPOKE interfaces freeze |
   131	| 4 (Thick Spokes a) | OD-02 (post-quantum JWT) | Must resolve before authz contract hardens |
   132	| 5 (Thick Spokes b) | OD-03 (soft naming collision), OD-04 (exemplar labels) | Docs cleanup before public API exposure |
	< truncated lines 133-295 >
   296	| **Quarterly Reconciliation** | Every 3 months | 3 days | Canonical tree diff, archive old versions, renumber if needed, plan next 2 quarters |
   297	
   298	**Total ceremony overhead:** ~3.5 hours/week during bets, ~0 hours during cooldown (except Ring Lock). For a 3-person team, this is ~4% of available time.
   299	
   300	---
   301	
   302	## 9. Risk management: The "Kill the Bet" rule
   303	
   304	Shape Up's most important contribution is **appetite-based scoping**: if the work doesn't fit the appetite, you don't extend — you reduce scope or kill.
   305	
   306	### 9.1 Kill triggers (Bet Architecture Review, Week 3)
   307	
   308	| Trigger | Action |
   309	|---------|--------|
   310	| CORE-02 still a stub at Week 3 of Bet 1 | **Kill Bet 1.** Descope to "CORE-02 only." Extend Bet 1 to 6 weeks *just for CORE-02*. Everything else moves to Bet 2. |
   311	| HUB-04 cannot resolve OD-02 (post-quantum JWT) | **Kill the crypto facet.** Ship ES256 (ADR-003) as interim. Move post-quantum to Future Scale. |
   312	| ISPOKE-23 dormant Pulse format undecided | **Kill ISPOKE-23 from Bet 5.** Ship without sagas. Move C3 patch (dormant Pulses) to Future Scale. |
   313	| Zero-Exposure Test fails at Week 2 of Bet 6 | **Kill Bet 6.** Do not ship BRIDGE-01 without it. Extend by 1 week (the **only** exception — security is non-negotiable). |
   314	| Performance targets missed in Bet 7 | **Kill the ESPOKE that missed.** Ship the others. Move the failing ESPOKE to a follow-up bet. |
   315	
   316	### 9.2 Rollback strategy
   317	
   318	**The honest gap:** Neither original model addressed rollback.
   319	
   320	**The fix:** Every Ring Lock produces a **rollback artifact**:
   321	
   322	| Artifact | Contents | Stored |
   323	|----------|----------|--------|
   324	| Ring snapshot | Git tag + container image digest + DB migration version | GitHub Releases + container registry |
   325	| Pulse state | Serialized dormant Pulses, queue depth, cache warmness | `CORE-19` snapshot table |
   326	| Config state | `HUB-01` feature flags, `HUB-21` tenant config | Sealed secret backup |
   327	
   328	**Rollback rule:** If Increment N fails Ring Lock, roll back to Increment N-1 snapshot. The team has 48 hours to fix or kill the bet. No "fix forward" during Ring Lock — that way lies production incidents.
   329	
   330	---
   331	
   332	## 10. Performance verification per ring
   333	
   334	**The honest gap:** STRUCTURE-09 exists but neither model referenced it.
   335	
   336	**The fix:** Every Ring Lock includes performance benchmarks:
   337	
   338	| Bet | Performance target | Method |
   339	|-----|-------------------|--------|
   340	| 1 (Core) | DI container resolves 1,000 bindings in < 10ms | `phpunit --group performance` on GitHub Actions runner |
   341	| 2 (Hub Critical) | HUB-04 authenticates 1,000 JWTs/sec | `wrk` against stub endpoint |
   342	| 3 (Hub Full) | HUB-15 health check returns in < 50ms at 100 RPS | `k6` load test |
   343	| 4–5 (Thick Spokes) | ISPOKE-01 admin panel renders in < 200ms | Lighthouse CI |
   344	| 6 (Inner Rim) | BRIDGE-01 rejects unauthorized Pulse in < 5ms | `wrk` with invalid JWT |
   345	| 7 (Thin Spokes) | ESPOKE-01 CMS serves 1,000 concurrent users, p95 < 500ms | `k6` against staging |
   346	| 8 (Hardening) | Full system: 10,000 RPS, p99 < 1s | Load test against production-like cluster |
   347	
   348	Performance targets are **provisional until Bet 7**. Before Bet 7, they are "best effort, measured, not enforced." From Bet 7 onward, they are **blocking**.
   349	
   350	---
   351	
   352	## 11. Production operations: The 9th increment (ongoing)
   353	
   354	**The honest gap:** Increment 8 ends at deployment, but SRE/oncall/incident response (ISPOKE-25!) needs a lifecycle too.
   355	
   356	**The fix:** AGRD defines an ongoing **9th increment** — not a bet, but a perpetual operating model:
   357	
   358	| Activity | Owner | Cadence |
   359	|----------|-------|---------|
   360	| On-call rotation | Engineer C (rotating) | Weekly |
   361	| Incident response | ISPOKE-25 (Incident Response Console) | Event-driven |
   362	| Security patching | Engineer A | Monthly |
   363	| Performance regression | Engineer B | Weekly (automated alert) |
   364	| OD triage | Rotating chair | Weekly ADR Review |
   365	| Quarterly reconciliation | Team | Every 3 months |
   366	| Annual architecture review | Team + external reviewer | Every 12 months |
   367	
   368	This is documented in `Architecture/OPERATIONS.md` (to be written during Bet 8 cooldown).
   369	
   370	---
   371	
   372	## 12. Comparison: AGRD vs. alternatives
   373	
   374	| Criterion | AGRD | Scrum | Waterfall | Pure Shape Up |
   375	|-----------|------|-------|-----------|---------------|
   376	| **Maps to Wheel architecture** | ✅ Rings as bets | ❌ Sprints ignore rings | ❌ One big bang | ⚠️ Bets don't know rings |
   377	| **Leverages existing lint** | ✅ `run.php --ring` | ❌ Lint is "debt" | ❌ No lint | ⚠️ No arch lint |
   378	| **Handles dependency DAG** | ✅ Ring sequence | ❌ Sprints pretend parallel | ✅ Planned but rigid | ⚠️ Appetite ignores DAG |
   379	| **Doc-first culture** | ✅ ADR gates | ❌ Stories are code-centric | ✅ Docs frozen early | ⚠️ Pitch is product-centric |
   380	| **3-person team fit** | ✅ Light rituals | ❌ Ceremony-heavy | ❌ Too heavy | ✅ Light rituals |
   381	| **Open Decisions tracked** | ✅ OD queue = backlog | ❌ Backlog is features | ❌ Change requests | ⚠️ No decision tracking |
   382	| **Interface stability** | ✅ Soft → Hard Freeze | ❌ Refactoring sprints | ✅ Frozen too early | ⚠️ Scope changes per bet |
   383	| **Security timing** | ✅ Moved left (Fix 1) | ❌ Per-sprint | ❌ Late phase | ❌ Silent |
   384	| **Performance verification** | ✅ Per-ring benchmarks | ❌ Sprint retro | ❌ End-only | ⚠️ Optional |
   385	| **Rollback strategy** | ✅ Ring snapshots | ❌ Revert PR | ❌ Big bang rollback | ❌ Not addressed |
   386	| **Bus factor mitigation** | ✅ Primary + reviewer | ❌ Solo ownership | ❌ Handoff docs | ❌ Not addressed |
   387

   409	
   410	## 14. The one anti-pattern to watch for
   411	
   412	> **Don't let the linter become the architect.**
   413	
   414	The linter (`run.php`) enforces decisions already made. It does not make decisions. If the team finds itself editing canonical docs to satisfy the linter — rather than editing the linter to reflect canonical decisions — the model is inverted.
   415	
   416	**The ADR Review is where decisions are made. The linter is the notary. The Ring Lock is the seal.**
   417	
   418	---
   419	
   420	## 15. Open questions (for AGRD v1.1)
   421	
   422	1. **Bet overlap:** Can Bet N+1 planning start during Bet N's cooldown? *(Recommended: Yes, planning only. No code.)*
   423	2. **HUB-31 exception:** If OD-01 is accepted mid-Bet 2, does Hub become 31 components? *(Recommended: No. HUB-31 ships in a later bet or cooldown.)*
   424	3. **External contributors:** How does a PR from an external contributor work? *(Recommended: Must reference an accepted ADR and pass all gates. No exceptions.)*
   425	4. **P0 bug during cooldown:** Break cooldown? *(Recommended: Yes, with emergency ADR. Cooldown resumes after fix.)*
   426	5. **Annual review:** Should AGRD itself be reviewed annually? *(Recommended: Yes, at Quarterly Reconciliation in Q4.)*

Core principle: Architecture is the backlog\n---\n> ## 2. Why Standard SDLCs Fail Here\n34c30,39\n< The backlog is not a list of features. You'd discover CORE-02 is wrong in month 11. |\n> | **Pure Scrum** | Sprints assume parallelizable stories. You can't put HUB-04 in Sprint 3 when CORE-02 won't land until Sprint 6. A 3-person team would drift without the ring boundaries forcing integration. |\n> | **Spiral** | Overkill. A PR without an ADR reference is a **logical failure** — you cannot implement what has not been decided.\n---\n> ## 3. The delivery unit: Ring Bet\n---\n> ## 4. The AGRD v2 Model\n49c54\n< A **Ring Bet** is a scoped commitment to deliver one Wheel ring to the fidelity bar defined in `AUTHORING_GUIDE.md`.\n---\n> ### 4.1 Core Principles\n51c56,62\n< ### 3.1 Ring Bet map\n---\n> 1. **Rings are the roadmap.** Each Wheel ring maps to one or more \"bets\" of 6 weeks. The dependency DAG enforces inside-out sequencing.\n> 2. **ADRs are the gates.** Every PR references an ADR (or marks itself \"ADR-exempt: implementation-only\"). The ADR Review is where decisions are made.\n> 3. **The linter is the notary, not the architect.** `run.php` enforces decisions; it doesn't make them. If the team edits canonical docs to satisfy the linter rather than the reverse, the model has inverted.\n> 4. **Pulse-Driven Demos.** Every Ring Lock demo is a live Pulse — `GET /healthz` → depth 6 → `200 OK`. No PowerPoint. Live Pulses or it didn't happen.\n> 5. **Tangential team flow.** 3 engineers work parallel *within* a ring (tangential), never *across* rings (radial). All 3 swarm CORE-02 in week 1; split into facets once it lands.\n> 6. **Security moves left.** BRIDGE-01's Authz, Audit, and Zero-Exposure facets are built incrementally across all bets — not bolted on at week 26.\n> 7. **Soft Freeze during build, Hard Freeze post-cutover.** Ring Lock applies a Soft Freeze (ADR-gated changes allowed) for Bets 1–8, transitioning to Hard Freeze (true SemVer) after production cutover.\n53,62c64\n< | Bet | Ring | Scope | Weeks | Entry Gate | Exit Gate (Ring Lock) |\n< |-----|------|-------|-------|------------|----------------------|\n< | 1 | **Core** | CORE-02→06, CORE-08→10, CORE-18 (booting kernel) | 6 | ADR-002 (PSR-11 container scope) accepted | `GET /healthz` returns 200; tenant lane isolation enforced; every rejection audited |\n< | 7 | **Thin Spokes + Outer Rim** | ESPOKE-01..15 + DEPLOY-01..04 | 8 | Bet 6 Ring Lock | Public API load test passes (STRUCTURE-09 targets); 15 ESPOKEs serve traffic; promotion pipeline (DEPLOY-04) deploys dev→staging→prod |\n< | 8 | **Hardening** | Chaos engineering, security audit, GDPR compliance, performance baseline, rollback verification | 4 | Bet 7 Ring Lock | Penetration test clean; audit chain integrity verified; RTO/RPO targets met; rollback to Bet 7 state demonstrated |\n---\n> ### 4.2 The Unified Cadence\n64c66,87\n< **Total: 48 weeks of bets + 7 weeks of cooldown = 55 weeks.**\n---\n> ```\n> Layer 1 (Macro): Radial Incremental\n>   Bet 1 (Core)              Weeks 1–6   + 1 cooldown\n>   Bet 2 (Hub Critical)      Weeks 8–13  + 1 cooldown\n>   Bet 3 (Hub Full)          Weeks 15–20 + 1 cooldown\n>   Bet 4 (Thick Spokes a)    Weeks 22–27 + 1 cooldown\n>   Bet 5 (Thick Spokes b)    Weeks 29–34 + 1 cooldown\n>   Bet 6 (Inner Rim)         Weeks 36–39 + parallel\n>   Bet 7 (Thin Spokes)       Weeks 36–45\n>   Bet 8 (Outer Rim)         Weeks 35–45 + 1 cooldown\n>   Bet 9 (Hardening)         Weeks 47–52\n> \n> Layer 2 (Micro, inside each bet):\n>   Weekly ADR review (30 min) — triage ODs for this ring\n>   Continuous PR flow — lint-gated, ADR-referenced\n>   Pulse-Driven Demo at Ring Lock — live Pulse, no slides\n>   Soft Freeze on interface — ADR-gated changes allowed\n>   Cross-repo Pulse contract tests — mandatory at Ring Lock\n>   Canonical doc version bump — STRUCTURE-XX vX.Y per ring\n> ```\n> \n> ### 4.3 The One Rule That Prevents Both Failure Modes\n66c89\n< ### 3.2 Why 6 weeks (±2) per ring\n---\n> > **Don't let the linter become the architect.** The ADR Review is where decisions are made. The linter is the notary. The Ring Lock is the seal.\n68,71c91,95\n< - **Shorter:** You can't deliver a ring with fidelity. The `AUTHORING_GUIDE.md` bar requires interface contracts, benchmark methodology, CI verification criteria, and dependency audit.\n< - **Longer:** The team loses momentum. A 3-person team needs a meaningful demo every 6–8 weeks.\n< - **6 weeks is the appetite, not the estimate.** If a ring isn't done in its allotted weeks, the bet is **killed** or **descoped** — never extended. This prevents the \"one more week\" death spiral.\n< - **Exceptions:** Bet 2 (Hub Critical) gets 8 weeks because 7 critical services with cross-dependencies need integration time. Bet 6 (Inner Rim) gets only 3 weeks because security was **moved left** — see §4.1.\n---\n> This prevents:\n> - Radial Incremental's risk: architecture drifting because there's no decision-making ritual.\n> - AGCA's risk: team editing canonical docs to satisfy the linter rather than the reverse.\n> \n> ---\n73c97\n< ### 3.3 Cooldowns: Mandatory 1-week between bets\n---\n> ## 5. It is why the canonical tree stays coherent while code accelerates.\n---\n> ### 5.1 Why Bets 6, 7, 8 Overlap\n> \n> Inner Rim hardening (Bet 6), Thin Spokes build (Bet 7), and Outer Rim pipeline (Bet 8) all run in parallel once Core + Hub + Thick Spokes are stable:\n> \n> - Bet 8 (Outer Rim) can start at week 35 because it depends on Core + Hub, not Thin Spokes.\n> - Bet 6 (Inner Rim) is a 3-week hardening bet, not a 6-week build — the facets were built incrementally.\n> - Bet 7 (Thin Spokes) is the longest of the three and gates the final integration.\n89c129\n< ## 4. Treat 9c as the start of permanent ops, not a finite bet.\n225c263\n< ## 6. The ADR queue: The only backlog\n---\n> ## 7. OD → ADR → Bet Mapping\n227c265\n< `OPEN-DECISIONS.md` is the backlog. Each OD is an ADR waiting to be written.\n---\n> Each Open Decision is the only backlog item that can become an ADR. Each ADR maps to a target bet where it must be resolved.\n229c267,274\n< ### 6.1 OD lifecycle\n---\n> | OD | Description | Resolves in Bet | Why This Bet |\n> |----|-------------|-----------------|--------------|\n> | OD-01 | HUB-31 real-time analytics — accept? | 3 (Hub Full) | Must decide before Hub interface Soft-Freezes |\n> | OD-02 | Post-quantum / algorithm-agile JWT — write ADR-012 | 6 (Inner Rim) | Must resolve before authz contract Hard-Freezes |\n> | OD-03 | Soft naming collision — process gap | 7 (Thin Spokes) | Docs cleanup before public API exposure |\n> | OD-04 | Two components labeled \"exemplar\" | 7 (Thin Spokes) | Docs cleanup before public API exposure |\n> | OD-05 | ISPOKE folder name vs INDEX component name | 4 (Thick Spokes a) | Must resolve before ISPOKE interfaces Soft-Freeze |\n> | OD-06 | ADR-010 opcache baseline — blocked on CORE-02 | 1 (Core) | Blocks performance verification of Core |\n231,234c276\n< ```\n< [Identified] → [Assigned owner] → [ADR draft] → [Weekly review] → [Accepted/Superseded/Deferred]\n<      ↑__________________________________________↓\n< ```\n---\n> ---\n236,240c278\n< - **Identified:** Any bet can surface a new OD. Recorded in `OPEN-DECISIONS.md` within 24 hours.\n< - **Assigned:** Weekly ADR review (30 min, rotating chair) assigns an owner and a **target bet** (not just a date).\n< - **ADR draft:** Owner writes the ADR in Nygard format. Must reference a `STRUCTURE-XX` section.\n< - **Weekly review:** Team reviews draft. Accepted → ratified. Superseded → linked to replacement. Deferred → moved to \"Future Scale\" (re-evaluated at Quarterly Reconciliation).\n< - **SLA:** 2 weeks from Identified to Accepted/Superseded/Deferred. No OD lives in limbo.\n---\n> ## 8. Ring Lock Ceremony\n242c280\n< ### 6.2 Current OD assignments\n---\n> Every Ring Lock is a formal ceremony with five components:\n244,251c282,296\n< | OD | Topic | Owner | Target Bet | Resolution path |\n< |----|-------|-------|------------|-----------------|\n< | OD-01 | HUB-31 real-time analytics | TBD | Bet 2 (Hub Critical) | Accept ADR-011 or defer to Future Scale |\n< | OD-02 | Post-quantum JWT signing | TBD | Bet 4 (Thick Spokes a) | Ship ES256 (ADR-003) as interim; move post-quantum to Future Scale |\n< | OD-03 | GraphQL (HUB-24) vs REST-only | TBD | Bet 3 (Hub Full) | Decision affects HUB-08 Gateway design |\n< | OD-04 | Multi-region tenancy | TBD | Bet 7 (Thin Spokes) | Affects DEPLOY-03 edge design |\n< | OD-05 | SuperPHP compiler vs Twig/Blade | TBD | Bet 4 (Thick Spokes a) | ADR-005 already leans SuperPHP; ratify or amend |\n< | OD-06 | BRIDGE-01 facet split (edge vs policy) | TBD | Bet 6 (Inner Rim) | Affects penetration policy implementation |\n---\n> 1. **Pulse-Driven Demo** — a live Pulse traverses the ring and returns. No slides.\n> 2. **Lint Verification** — `run.php` passes with zero findings for the rings built so far.\n> 3. **Cross-repo Pulse Contract Tests** — all 6-tuples are consistent across polyrepos touching the ring.\n> 4. **ADR Ratification** — any ODs assigned to this bet are resolved and ratified as ADRs.\n> 5. **Interface Soft-Freeze** — the ring's public interface is Soft-Frozen (ADR-gated changes allowed during build; Hard-Freeze at production cutover).\n> \n> ### 8.1 Ring Lock Risk Review (5 Questions)\n> \n> | Question | If \"No\" → |\n> |----------|-----------|\n> | Can a Pulse traverse this ring and return? | Extend bet by 1 week; fix before proceeding |\n> | Is the interface Soft-Frozen (ADR-gated)? | Freeze it now; document exceptions |\n> | Does `run.php` pass with zero findings? | Fix misattributions before outer-ring work starts |\n> | Can the next ring begin with zero blockers? | Identify blockers; swarm or descope |\n> | Is team velocity sustainable? | Adjust scope; never compress quality |\n255c300\n< ## 7. PR gates (every repo, every PR)\n---\n> ## 9. Unified Concepts (Cross-Reference Table)\n257c302,310\n< ### 7.1 Automated gates (CI)\n---\n> | From Radial Incremental | From AGCA | Unified in AGRD v2 |\n> |------------------------|-----------|---------------------|\n> | Ring Lock freezes interfaces | ADR ratification at merge | **Ring Lock Ceremony** — demo + lint + ADR ratify + interface freeze |\n> | Tangential team flow (parallel within ring) | Facet ownership | **Tangential ownership** — 3 engineers own facets (Identity, Data, Gateway), rotate every 2 bets |\n> | Pulse-driven demos | Contract tests | **Pulse contract tests** — cross-repo 6-tuple verification is mandatory |\n> | Dependency DAG sequencing | ADR queue | **OD → ADR → Bet** — Open Decisions are the only backlog; each maps to a target bet |\n> | — | 6-week bets + 2-week cooldown | **Ring Bet** — 6 weeks per ring, never extended (kill or descope); cooldowns are 1 week |\n> | — | `run.php` as notary | **Automated + human gates** — linter enforces decisions; ADR Review makes them |\n> | Hard SemVer freeze at Ring Lock | — | **Soft Freeze during build, Hard Freeze post-cutover** |\n259,266c312\n< | Gate | Tool | Failure mode |\n< |------|------|-------------|\n< | Architecture lint (ring-scoped) | `run.php --ring=<current>` | Non-zero exit; PR blocked |\n< | Reference existence | `run.php` | `CORE-09` cited for crypto → fail (Pattern A) |\n< | Tier direction | `run.php` | Deploy component in Core's Upward list → fail (ADR-004) |\n< | Unit tests | PHPUnit | < 80% coverage on modified files → fail |\n< | Pulse contract tests | Custom (cross-repo) | Pulse 6-tuple drift between consumer/producer → fail |\n< | Performance benchmark | `phpunit --group performance` | STRUCTURE-09 targets not met → fail (Bet 7+) |\n---\n> ---\n268c314\n< ### 7.2 Human gates (PR review)\n---\n> ## 10. Here's my actual take, and there's a decisive piece of evidence, not just a preference.

**I checked the OD tables against the live `OPEN-DECISIONS.md` rather than trusting either document's claim to be current. They don't agree with each other on what the 6 Open Decisions even *are* — and only one of them matches reality.**

| OD | Real repo (`OPEN-DECISIONS.md`, current) | File1 (v1.0) | File2 (v2) |
|---|---|---|---|

File2's OD-03 through OD-06 match the live repo almost word for word. File1's don't match at all — and worse, **File1 contradicts itself**: its own §3.3 says "OD-06 = CORE-02 opcache baseline," then its own §6.2 says "OD-06 = BRIDGE-01 facet split." Two different topics, same number, same document. That's not a stale reference, that's an internal inconsistency in the artifact itself — exactly the class of bug this entire session exists to catch, now showing up in the delivery-planning layer instead of the architecture layer.

That's not a stylistic preference between two reasonable takes. It means File1's entire "Fix 3: OD resolution embedded in increments" — one of its six headline contributions — is scaffolding built on decisions that don't currently exist. File2 is the one actually looking at the ground truth.

**But File2 isn't clean either — it has a real scheduling bug its own author didn't catch:**

Its headline "Fix 2" (Bets 6/7/8 run in parallel, saving ~10 weeks) requires all 3 engineers each solo-owning an entire ring simultaneously for a 10-week stretch (weeks 35/36–45). That directly collides with two things the same document states elsewhere:
- Its own admitted Gap 4: *"3 people, 96 blueprints — if Engineer A is sick during Bet 6, can B or C cover?"* Answer, given the schedule it just proposed: no. Everyone's pinned solo to a different ring.
- Its own facet-rotation principle (§10.3): rotation-for-cross-training is meaningless during a stretch where nobody has slack to pair.

There's also a smaller literal collision: Bet 8 starts week 35, which is also Bet 5's cooldown week — the same week is simultaneously "rest and reconcile" and "start a new ring," for a 3-person team where those are the same people.

**Both share one unexamined assumption, inherited from neither Kimi nor Z.ai checking it against actual code:** Bet 6 shrinking to 3–4 weeks depends entirely on the Authz/Audit facets *actually* landing cleanly as incremental byproducts of Bets 1–5. There's no `BRIDGE-01` implementation in the repo yet — only `CORE-01` (orchestrator) and `CORE-03` (event-dispatcher) have real code, confirmed earlier this session. That's a planning bet, stated as if it were already de-risked, in both documents.

**My actual recommendation, not just critique:** formalize File2 as the ADR-013/014 base — its grounding in the live repo state is the deciding factor — but fix the parallel-bet structure before ratifying. Either (a) drop to 2-way parallel (Bet 8 solo-tracked by whichever engineer isn't rotating through Bet 6/7, with the third providing review capacity across both rather than owning a third simultaneous ring), accepting a few weeks back onto the 52, or (b) keep 3-way parallel but add an explicit "Negative consequence" to the ADR-013 draft acknowledging zero bus-factor coverage for that stretch, so it's a stated trade-off instead of a silent one. I'd take (a) — the whole point of this SDLC is that architecture drift gets caught before it's expensive; an unstated coverage gap in the delivery plan is the same failure mode aimed at itself.

**[ASSISTANT]**

That's not a scheduling tweak — that invalidates the load-bearing assumption under both documents, more fundamentally than the parallel-bet bug I found. Every "3-person" framing in File2 (explicit) and File1 (implicit in its bet-week sizing) assumed three people who could each independently own architecture decisions and implementation for a full ring. You have one. The other two aren't engineering capacity that got mis-scheduled — they're a different kind of capacity that neither document accounts for at all.

Mechanically, this means:

- **All parallel engineering bets are off the table, full stop** — not "risky," void. Bets 6/7/8 running simultaneously required 3 people each solo-owning a ring. With one tech lead, everything engineering-side is sequential, no exceptions.
- **The per-bet week estimates themselves are suspect**, not just the parallel structure — File2's own "3 people, 96 blueprints" framing implies those 6-week bet durations assumed 3-person collaborative throughput. Solo-with-AI-agents (matching how you actually work — Cline, Jules) isn't 1/3 as fast, since there's no coordination tax to pay, but it's not equivalent either: AI agents multiply *implementation* throughput, they don't substitute for the *review and judgment* capacity that was the real thing 3 people provided. That's a bottleneck no amount of agent leverage removes, because it's still one person's attention gating every merge.

The genuinely good news, though: marketer and media aren't idle in this model — they're a **second, real, non-blocking track**, just not the one either document imagined. Concretely, and this connects straight back to the splash-screen/microcopy conversation: marketer owns copy through `HUB-13` (every string as a translation key, even English-only — means their work is never blocked waiting on you) plus `ESPOKE-05` marketing page content; media owns visual direction feeding `HUB-26`'s theme tokens plus whatever demo/video content the dev portal or public site eventually wants. Neither of those gates the engineering critical path or waits on it — they can run continuously from week 1.

Rather than hand you a new fake precise total (that would repeat the exact mistake this whole session exists to catch — an unmeasured number stated as fact), I'd restructure the methodology itself:

1. **Walking skeleton before ring completionism.** Instead of "finish Core, then finish Hub, then finish Spokes" (fine for 3 people, brutal for 1 — you don't see a working system until very late), Milestone 0 is Exemplar One made real: `CORE-02` just enough to unblock, one Hub service, `BRIDGE-01`, one Internal Spoke, one External Spoke — a genuine end-to-end round trip. Surfaces integration surprises immediately instead of at week 40, when they're catastrophically expensive to fix alone.
2. **Calibrate from Milestone 0, don't project up front.** Measure your actual solo-with-agents velocity completing it, then size Bets 2+ from that real number — same principle as Governance Rule 2 (no benchmark claim without a stated method), just applied to the schedule instead of the code.
3. **Keep what's actually good in both documents** — ADR-gating, the kill-the-bet triggers, lint enforcement, cooldown weeks (arguably more necessary solo, not less — nobody else catches drift if you skip one). Adopt File2's OD table as-is, since it's the one that's actually current. Discard File1's OD table and its parallel-bet structure entirely.
